<meta>This page continues the Ra’avad’s mystical introduction to the Book of Creation. Having established the relationship between the divine "Voice" and physical "matter" on the previous page, he now turns to the mechanics of pregnancy and birth, viewing them as a reflection of the Sefirot (divine emanations). He focuses on how the spiritual timing of birth determines a soul’s destiny.</meta>

# Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

For satisfaction and from the **Understanding** <gloss>Binah</gloss>, the "greenness" within the embroidery <note>Likely referring to the intricate formation of the embryo</note> acts like the opening of her womb, bringing forth below. It is like the "potential matter" <term>Hyle; the primordial substance from which all things are formed</term> that is planted to give birth to the hidden child, waiting for its time to be born until its full capacity arrives. 

We learn that the pain of childbirth is one of the ten curses placed upon Eve, stemming from the aspect of "a hanging grape" <note>A metaphorical description of the embryo's precarious state</note>. In the time of the Garden of Eden, there was a wellspring of "Strong Knowledge," which allowed for eternal life—except for the bird of **Kindness** <gloss>Hesed</gloss> named the *Chul* <note>The Phoenix, which according to legend lives for a thousand years and then regenerates</note>, as it is written: *And I shall multiply my days as the Chul* <note>Job 29:18</note>. Therefore, all living things were cursed because of Eve with the pain of birth and with death. Every species of living creature has its own specific gestation time, as explained in the **Torah** and in **Tractate Bekhorot** <note>The Talmudic volume dealing with the laws of the firstborn and animal gestation</note>. The gestation time of each is the period required for the physical matter to reach its completion.

And behold, when the time of birth arrives, the "Rock of Strength" ascends, and the time of "the end of the forces" begins. This corresponds to the seven types of pure qualities: **Crown** <gloss>Atarah/Malkhut</gloss>, **Foundation** <gloss>Yesod</gloss>, **Victory** <gloss>Netzach</gloss>, **Splendor** <gloss>Hod</gloss>, **Beauty** <gloss>Tiferet</gloss>, **Kindness** <gloss>Hesed</gloss>, and **Strength** <gloss>Gevurah</gloss>. In this mystical system, "Diyon" represents **Beauty** <gloss>Tiferet</gloss>. The exchange of "Diyon" is **Kindness** <gloss>Hesed</gloss>, for there is a distinction between the portion of the children of Jacob and the portion of the children of Esau.

The "greenness" mentioned earlier comes from the power of *Dan shall be a serpent by the way* <note>Genesis 49:17</note>. This represents the completion of the forces that strike through the hand of Dan, who seals the judgment for the child to be born. As our sages said: *Against your will you are formed, against your will you are born, and against your will you live* <note>Pirkei Avot 4:22</note>. This is what is meant by "the child is born through the exchange of forces." 

Behold, birth is through the power of the Lord, for He is the one who "unties the birth-pangs of the mother." We learn that in His greatness, at the moment He sends the child down to be born below, **Wisdom** <gloss>Chokhmah</gloss> is completed from above and departs from the **Kingdom** <gloss>Malkhut</gloss>. This is the power of the "Dream of Kindness," referring to the Lord of Birth. At the time that **Wisdom** <gloss>Chokhmah</gloss> departs, it descends into the "Seventy" <note>The seventy celestial princes or nations</note> and revolves around the "Thirteen" <note>The thirteen attributes of mercy</note>. From there, it receives all the future events destined for the child. 

Therefore, it is proper for you to contemplate this: a person who writes an amulet to speed up a birth before its natural time is effectively killing two forces. Sometimes this causes the child to be born on "evil days" and brings about great misfortunes, one worse than the other. These "destroyers" are the opposite of the "Seed of Jacob." This can be understood through a parable: a fetus is destined to be born on a certain day when the **Moon** <gloss>Atarah</gloss> is favorable. But if one uses "the copper serpent" <note>Magic or forbidden arts</note> to force the birth, they have uprooted the natural decree. By bringing the child out before the Moon has reached its station, they exchange a good destiny for a flawed one. Such a child is born at a time when its life-force is cancelled, often leading to death in a few days. The person who speeds this up "cleanses" the birth by force, but they have stolen the decree of the Creator and killed the living soul by bringing it forth out of its time. They may cause the child to lose certain wealth or a specific destiny. In short, such a person disrupts all the **Orders of Creation**. This is sufficient for anyone who understands the depth of this matter.

Furthermore, one who brings forth wealth <note>original: "Hon"; potentially a metaphor for the soul/seed</note> should do so according to the Torah. One should count the days of pregnancy and know the time of birth. The explanation is: the one who is "planted" to give birth should count the months backward to know at which degree or level the conception began. This is the order of the count:

Know that the time of birth depends on the number of days of pregnancy. There is a count for "conception" and a count for "gestation." The number is 278 days, or sometimes 273 days <note>A common Talmudic calculation for nine months of pregnancy</note>. The sign for this is "lacking a night from conception." Therefore, when the birth begins, you should count back 273 days from the time of birth. If the constellation associated with **Understanding** <gloss>Binah</gloss> is found there, you will know the true time of birth. If she did not immerse <note>in the Mikvah/ritual bath</note> at that time, but rather months before, you count 278 days. If at that time she also did not immerse, count 287 days. This is how you measure according to the "View of Understanding," which acts to "hold back" and "bring near." Therefore, all those who are conceived through the extended blessings of **Understanding** <gloss>Binah</gloss> have the power to "hold the drop" in the womb until it is ready. This is called "Religion/Law" <note>Dath</note> because it constricts and holds and measures.

->Sefer Yetzirah 3 4 6 7<-

<summary>In this passage, the Ra'avad explores the mystical and astrological dimensions of pregnancy and birth, linking the physical process to the Sefirot. He warns against using magical amulets to induce labor, arguing that disrupting the divinely ordained timing of birth ruins a child's destiny and violates the cosmic order.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the Ra’avad’s mystical commentary, moving from the general theory of gestation and "potential matter" to specific examples of how the Divine sustains life and the biological-spiritual process of digestion and birth. It links the behavior of animals like lions, ravens, and goats to deeper Kabbalistic structures.</meta>

# Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

The path of the ascent. This is the secret of the *young lions roaring for prey, seeking their food from God* <note>Psalm 104:21</note>, meaning their sustenance. After he mentioned the "Face of the Lion" <note>One of the four faces of the Divine Chariot in Ezekiel’s vision</note> and the order of its nourishment through the **Understanding** <gloss>Binah</gloss> appropriate to it, he returned to mention the branches of the "Face of the Eagle," which includes every bird. He says that the nature of the raven is so cruel that it possesses no mercy at all. Therefore, when it gives birth to its young, it sees them as white <note>According to Midrash, raven chicks are born with white feathers, and the parents, thinking they are not theirs, abandon them</note>. It refuses to provide for them or gather them in the snow. What does the Holy One, Blessed be He, do? He prepares little gnats for them, which fly into the mouths of the chicks, and from these they are nourished. This is what is written: *He gives to the beast its food, and to the young ravens which cry* <note>Psalm 147:9</note>.

At that time, the dog—which is a "beast" <term>Behemah; often refers to domestic animals, but here used broadly</term>—is also provided for. Know that creatures do not usually provide for dogs; therefore, the Holy One, Blessed be He, created a special quality for the dog: everything it eats remains in its intestines for three days before it is digested. This reinforces the verse *He gives to the beast its food*. And regarding the raven: *Who provides for the raven its prey, when its young ones cry unto God, and wander for lack of food?* <note>Job 38:41</note>. The translation <note>The Aramaic Targum</note> of this is: "Who prepares for the raven its food when its young cry before God and wander because of the lack of food?"

It is well known that all food transforms according to the one who eats it. Just as the food... <unclear>illegible</unclear>... is appropriate to add to the body, some of it becomes the animal's flesh, and through the mercy of the "fluids" it becomes waste, and from the waste comes energy <note>Literally "strength"</note>. Through mercy, the food is transformed into milk and then returns to be blood. Our labor to break down the food results in it becoming separated into various components—the liver, the blood, and the "residue" in the gallbladder. 

You must discern that every likeness is a "language" and a "speaker"; they all pass through like "children of change" <note>original: "bnei chalof"; a reference to Proverbs 31:8, meaning those who are transient or passing away</note> in their ascent and descent. Everything happens by the "Judgment of the Worker" <note>Divine Law</note>, by the mouth of the Lord they shall rest and by the mouth of the Lord they shall journey. Therefore, it is possible for a pure bird to be strengthened by the "breaking" <note>The breakdown of matter</note>. How so? The bird is born from an egg, and that egg returns to be a pure bird, a living soul in the cycles. 

Specifically, before God, this is **Kindness** <gloss>Hesed</gloss>: that the **Understanding** <gloss>Binah</gloss> transforms the fluid into milk, and it is absorbed into all the 248 limbs and all the sinews. Then it returns to be the guardian of the "form of the sun" <note>A mystical reference to the vital life force or the soul's radiance</note>. Thus, "its children cry out to God" in their specific essence. When does this happen? When it happens to them that the eater is superior to the food. If the food is "consumed" by the eater, it rises. But if the food is dangerous or heavy, those who walk toward it fall. 

If the animal is far from being elevated, it still remains under the "judgment of death," without any ascent to the higher levels. For there, the "Force of the Food" <note>The spiritual essence within physical nourishment</note> performs its work, bringing forth the effort to ascend. This is the secret of why they ate with the "breaking" <note>Perhaps referring to the transition from vegetarianism to eating meat</note>, for it is permitted to eat the meat of the animal. In the **Torah**, it is sometimes permitted to eat meat and sometimes forbidden. If the food is "consumed" by the person, it is thickened and rolled from level to level until it reaches the highest "faraway" level. This is why it says *they wander for lack of food*. For those who are "consumed" wander in their cycles—why? For the "children of food." If they are strengthened, they return to "nothingness" <note>Ayin; the Divine source</note> and do not go toward loss or descent. Therefore, the food wanders according to the law of the "children of change." This is the secret of *its young ones cry unto God*—if the eater is superior to the food—and *they wander for lack of food*.

*Do you know the time when the wild goats of the rock bring forth?* <note>Job 39:1</note>. This "Ya'eleh" <term>Ya'el; Nubian Ibex or Mountain Goat</term> is a male mountain goat, and its name in the language of the **Torah** is *Dishon*. The translation of *Dishon* is "Ya'ela." It is included in the "Faces of the Living Creatures" <note>The Hayot/Angels of the Chariot</note>. It is a known tradition that when the mountain goat is ready to give birth, she is compassionate. God summons a dragon <note>original: "Drakon"; referring to a serpent</note> for her, which bites her womb, and she immediately gives birth. If the dragon were to arrive one moment too early or one moment too late, she would not be able to survive, because her womb is extremely narrow and the offspring cannot emerge...

<summary>The Ra'avad explains how God provides for all creatures—even "cruel" ravens and dogs—through unique biological traits. He then details a mystical view of digestion as a process of spiritual elevation and describes the miraculous birth of the mountain goat involving a "dragon."</summary>
<keywords>Ravens, Dogs, Digestion, Womb, Mountain Goat, Dragon, Hesed, Binah, Elevation of Matter, Divine Providence</keywords>

--- Page 32 ---
<meta>This page continues the Ra’avad’s mystical commentary on the wild animals described in the Book of Job (chapter 39), moving from the raven and the dog to the "donkey" (Chamur), the "wild ass" (Arod), and the "wild ox" (Re’em). These creatures are treated as allegories for the struggle between the material world, the "Other Side" (Sitra Achra), and the eventual dominion of the Divine Kingship (Malchut).</meta>

# Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

### Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

The purpose of this "Good Psalm" <note>Referring to the poetic section of Job 39</note> is to instruct man regarding the means through which our books are sent. Regarding the "donkey" <note>original: *chamur*</note>, the Sages of blessed memory spoke of it as "the donkey I built" to be a speaker in its rebellion against civilization and its affairs. Therefore, amidst the rushes of—God forbid—rebellion against civilization and the habituation of the wilderness, there is a "donkey of the desert" <note>An "Arod" or wild ass</note>, characterized as a "trapped and fleeting spirit" <note>original: *kefat shelefat ruach*</note>.

The "bonds of the wild ass" <note>Job 39:5</note> refer to the eight levels of birds that dwell in the hidden paths of the wild ass. The essence of the wild ass is purely of the wilderness—it is "the donkey I built," turning away to become a creature of the desert. Similarly, the Sages of blessed memory distinguished between a "donkey" and a "wild ass" <note>In Jewish law, they are different species</note>. Its nature is to dwell in the deserts, bound by the chains and bonds of the wild ass. Who is it that can open and loosen these bonds of the wild ass to return it to Man? The Aramaic translation says: *Who has set the donkey free, and who has loosened the bonds of the wild ass?* <note>original Aramaic: *man katar mdora bar chorin umosrei aruda hachun sharei*</note>.

*Whose home I have made the wilderness, and the salt land his dwelling* <note>Job 39:6</note>. This explains that the dwelling of the wild ass is "fleeting." My judgments were sent to the wilderness and our drink was set toward the "salt land" <note>barren earth</note> in order to return it to Man. For a "salt land" is not inhabited, and thus it becomes like a lonely shrub in the desert that does not see when good comes. This is also the Aramaic translation: *In the desert, a salt land that is not inhabited.*

This is the secret of the **Kingship** <gloss>Malchut</gloss> being described as *poor and riding on a donkey, and on a colt, the foal of a donkey* <note>Zechariah 9:9</note>. For all those who are "spread out" <note>The external, wild forces</note> will eventually live under its dominion. This is translated as: *I have placed his house in the desert and his dwelling in a land that is not inhabited.*

*He scorns the tumult of the city; he does not hear the shouts of the driver* <note>Job 39:7</note>. Behold, it is in its nature to rebel against the **Kingship** <gloss>Malchut</gloss> and not to listen to the voice of the taskmaster or the voice of the ruler. If so, in what way is "return" written concerning it? Only when it is before Man, until it accepts upon itself the "yoke of the kingdom." As the translation says: *He laughs at the noise of the city, and the shouts of the driver he does not hear.*

*He ranges the mountains for his pasture, and he searches after every green thing* <note>Job 39:8</note>. Behold, the "fleeing one" and the "wild ass" travel by foot to be spies and scouts of the city. This refers to the judgments of **Wisdom** <gloss>Chokhmah</gloss>, **Understanding** <gloss>Binah</gloss>, and **Beauty** <gloss>Tiferet</gloss>. It is all for the sake of praise and debate. This is the secret of "and he smelled her from afar." Thus the donkey is envisioned as a "girded horse."

This is the secret of "taking the prize and riding for the kings of the earth." And *after every green thing he searches*—this refers to the **Green Line** <term>Kav Yarok; a mystical boundary mentioned in the Zohar and Sefer Yetzirah that surrounds the world and from which darkness emanates</term> which surrounds the entire world from every side. As the translation says: *And the girded crown explores the mountains for its pasture, seeking every green herb.*

*Will the wild ox be willing to serve you? Will he spend the night at your manger?* <note>Job 39:9</note>. This means: Know that all domestic animals, wild beasts, and birds are subjugated to Man. Wherever the "spirit" <note>Ruach</note> intends to go, they go, and they do not turn as they go, for they live from the height of Man. Therefore, the fear and dread of Man is upon every living thing. All of this is by divine decree. But the **Wild Ox** <note>original: *Re'em*</note>, because its portion is so great, rebels against Man. This is why it asks: *Will the wild ox be willing to serve you?* This means: will he be willing to pass through the judgment of "transient beings" <note>original: *bnei chalof*</note> to accept a yoke upon his neck, just as a servant serves his master?

Similarly, *will he spend the night at your manger?* For whoever takes from Man, his soul clings to him. Therefore, when he dies, his strength and being are gathered to him. It is written: *May it be rolled by my hand.* The secret here involves the "trapped and fleeting spirit." Through him, the "spirit is drawn out" and rolled. This is the secret of *will he spend the night at your manger?*—to make a covenant with you and to be subjugated according to the law of transient beings. But the wild ox does not desire to work for a human being, nor to turn toward him, so that no benefit shall reach Man from him. As the translation says: *Is the wild ox willing to serve you? Will he spend the night at your stable?*

*Can you bind the wild ox in the furrow with ropes? Or will he harrow the valleys after you?* <note>Job 39:10</note>. This is a difficult matter! Can you truly tie the wild ox in a furrow that has been separated to spread the seed? *With ropes* <note>original: *avoto*</note>—this means by using the strongest of the animals. *Will he harrow the valleys after you?* Meaning: can you use him to plow so that he returns the valleys to Man?

<summary>This page provides a Kabbalistic commentary on Job 39, interpreting the wild donkey and the wild ox as symbols of material forces and the "Other Side" that rebel against spiritual authority. It explains how these wild forces are eventually brought under the "yoke" of the Divine Kingship (Malchut) through the mystery of the "Green Line" and the messianic "riding on a donkey."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the Ra’avad’s mystical commentary on the wild creatures of Job 39. While the previous page focused on the "wild ass" (Arod) as a symbol of the untamed spirit, this page transitions into "mystical embryology," using the gestation of animals and the imagery of Job to explain the development of the human fetus and the descent of the soul through the Sefirot (divine emanations).</meta>

# Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

From the expression "his eye was not dim," <note>original: *lo khata eino* (Deuteronomy 34:7)</note> it is explained to mean that his teeth were not wrinkled. <note>The Ra'avad interprets physical vitality through the state of the teeth/jaw, a common medieval physiological theory</note>. Consider how much strength there is in the "extra understanding" <term>Binah: the divine attribute of Understanding and structure</term>. The seed, while it is up to thirty days old, is later governed by the power of Kindness <term>Chesed: the attribute of Mercy and expansion</term> and is revealed; it expels from itself things that are not fit to be rectified below. And the youth, etc., is a spark that awakens the 248 limbs <note>In Jewish tradition, the human body is composed of 248 limbs and 365 sinews</note> and various revelations.

After thirty days, the power of Strength <term>Gevurah: the attribute of Judgment and restriction</term> governs it, etc. Then comes the wrinkling and the "wing-like" movement, etc., and the "natural axis" spreads through all 248 limbs. It nullifies the "will for flesh" until it returns to flesh, and the "will for revelation," and [the will] for grapes to grapes, and for "gamnot" to "gamnot," <note>likely referring to botanical or anatomical structures</note> and so for every single companion [limb].

Afterward, the life of the "desire-power" is removed from it, along with the power of the multitudes and strength, and all their connections are established upon their base until the completion of thirty [more] days. After that, the power of Victory <term>Nezach</term> and the strength of the hearts rule over it for another thirty days. Afterward, the power of Splendor <term>Hod</term> is clarified, and they are tied with a strong and mighty knot and become sanctified. 

Afterward, the Foundation <term>Yesod</term> rules over it, and within it is the power of the Moon. Therefore, if it is born in the eighth month <note>original: *ch"h* - Chodesh HaShemini</note>, it is because the Foundation is the "Living God" <note>original: *El Chai*</note> that breathes life, being the Crown <term>Atarah: the final Sefirah, Malchut</term>. The secret is that "the Righteous One" (Foundation) is exactly opposite the "Crown," and the letter *Kof* <note>The letter Kof represents the "back of the head" or "monkeys," often associated with the transition between holy and unholy</note>—these are what bring forth the "creeping things."

Because the "Walking" <note>The higher soul's movement</note> serves in the Upper World, and the "Palate" <note>The physical senses</note> serves in the Lower World, it desires to cling to Strength and to separate like the Intellectual Soul from the murky body. Therefore, if a woman gives birth in the "eighth attribute" <note>the 8th month</note>, Heaven forbid <note>original: *ch"v*</note>, who will give a soul to this murky body? Therefore, those of the "kingly" power die. But if she gives birth in the "ninth attribute," which is associated with Kindness (*Chesed*), the child shall live, as it is said: "Life and favor You have granted me, and Your providence has preserved my spirit" <note>Job 10:12</note>. 

According to this order, all the days of gestation for every species are spread out, for their gestation days are divided by seven. Therefore, the Sages said: "If you know the time of birth, you know the time of conception; if you know the time of conception, you know the time of birth." This is the wisdom of the "months of the womb" from the Moon, as in: "Do you know the time when the mountain goats give birth?" <note>Job 39:1</note>. This is what the Aramaic translates as: *Will you see the months of those that go forth, and will you know the peace of their birth?* 

"They bow down, they bring forth their young, they cast out their pains" <note>Job 39:3</note>. Know that everyone who departs from the King is like one who flees, yet he bows to the King and takes permission from Him and accepts His decree. So too the soul, at the time it departs from the King, the Lord, with its powers—it bows down, it and all its companions, which are: 
- The Lower Soul <term>Nefesh: associated with the World of Action</term>
- The Spirit <term>Ruach: associated with the attribute of Beauty/Tiferet</term>
- The Higher Soul <term>Neshamah: associated with Understanding/Binah</term>
- The Living Soul <term>Chayah: associated with Wisdom/Chokhmah</term> 
- The Singular Soul <term>Yechidah: associated with the Crown/Keter</term> 

And its powers—at that time, Wisdom bows to give birth. And when the "Supernal Wisdom" <term>Chokhmah</term> cleaves <note>original: *tiflach*</note>, the knots of the "cleavings" are the Crown, Foundation, and Splendor, and they are for all the "Wisdoms of the ones who go forth"—"a joyful mother of children" <note>Psalm 113:9</note>. 

"Behold, they cast out their pains." This means the child is born and they become "mothers" at the time of birth. This is: "they bow down, they bring forth their young, they cleave their pangs, they cast out their pains." This means: "they cleave their young" because the one giving birth breaks through and tears the placenta <note>original: *salya*</note> in which it lies, and immediately "they cast out their pains." This means: the time to give birth arrives, and her labor pains are removed from her. This is what the Aramaic translates: *They are bent like purses, cleaving in their transition, arranged...* 

Just as all these attributes are rectified before the "Pangs" <note>meaning the birth process</note>, so too are they rectified before their "companions"—the Face of the Ox, the Face of the Eagle, and every line and "pen" between them. They go, but "they do not go to them." And when the children become strong and hold onto their King: "Behold, they go and increase." This means they grow and increase like the "secret of gestation." For all their attributes are directed toward Wisdom, for life and life, and upon the face of the "Pangs." Because the "Pang" is the holy level from which it comes. Behold, they are finished with Kindness and strengthened in the Lord and His portions. Their power is from the "harsh sources" that deserve excision <term>Karet: spiritual soul-cutting</term>, but they are crushed in the "rebellious exchanges" and will not turn back to them again.

This is what the Aramaic translates: *They are strengthened in their children, they acquire through their gestation, they go forth and do not return to them.* 

"Who has sent the wild ass free, and the bonds of the wild ass who has loosened?" <note>Job 39:5</note>. It is known that the "wild ass" <note>original: *pere*</note> is a cord/form of Kindness (*Chesed*), which is attributed to the Sea above all, and he will be a "wild man" <note>original: *pere adam* - Gen 16:12</note>. If so, they are prayers in "bloom" <note>a pun on *pere* (wild ass) and *perach* (flower/bloom)</note>; "his hand against everyone and everyone's hand against him," and he is bound in the bond of the Covenant—everything is a bloom.

וכעת <note>Catchword: And now</note>

<summary>The Ra’avad interprets the physiological and mystical development of a fetus in 30-day cycles corresponding to the Sefirot, eventually linking the birth imagery in Job 39 to the soul's descent into the physical body.</summary>
<keywords>Gestation, Sefirot, Job 39, Soul levels (Nefesh, Ruach, Neshamah), Mystical Embryology, Ra’avad, Kindness (Chesed), Strength (Gevurah)</keywords>

--- Page 34 ---
<meta>This page continues the Ra’avad’s mystical and physiological commentary on the Book of Job. Following the discussion on embryology and the development of the soul's powers, the text now turns to the symbolic descriptions of the ostrich (the "lacking wisdom" bird) and the war horse from Job chapter 39. The Ra’avad uses the Aramaic Targum (an ancient Jewish translation/paraphrase) to deepen the explanation of these creatures' natures.</meta>

# Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

...not in vain; he kisses without fear. <note>original: *yishak beli pakhad*. The commentator plays on the word *yis’haq* (laughs) and *yishak* (kisses/touches), suggesting the horse’s bold contact with danger.</note> This represents the boldness of the horse, which does not fear to engage in struggle and use its strength for its own benefit. It does not judge based on who is weak, nor by what is good, nor by force, for its past battles are remembered by it. Therefore, for the most part, **it laughs without fear**, without hesitation. This is as the Targum <note>The ancient Aramaic translation of the Bible</note> translates: "that it mocks at the collision, for it does not fear the emptiness of the drawing of the sword."

For God "deprived her of wisdom and did not allot her a share of understanding." <note>Job 39:17, referring to the ostrich</note> The explanation is that the ostrich’s <note>Hebrew: *sevakhat ha-sus*, though likely referring here to the *Renanim* or ostrich mentioned in the biblical context</note> lack of wisdom means she was not given a portion of **Binah** <term>Binah: the divine attribute of structured understanding</term>. Therefore, the rooster is called *Sekhwi* <note>In Jewish liturgy, God gives the *Sekhwi* (rooster/intellect) the ability to distinguish between day and night</note>, and accordingly, there is life in its movement and mercy, as the Targum translates: "the rooster's intellect" to merit its wisdom, "and He did not allot to her" of its understanding. **"When she lifts herself on high, she scorns the horse and its rider"** <note>Job 39:18</note>—the explanation is that she flies and flutters toward the heights, mocking all the chariots that would try to snatch her away, as the Targum translates: "In the time when she flies high in the heavens, she mocks the horse and its riders."

### The War Horse

**"Do you give the horse his might? Do you clothe his neck with a mane?"** <note>Job 39:19</note> The explanation is that the horse possesses all might, and its inheritance comes from the Kingdom, as the Targum translates: "Is it by your permission that you give the horse strength, or do you clothe his neck with power?"

**"Do you make him leap like a locust? His majestic snorting is terrible."** <note>Job 39:20</note> Do you make him flee? Do you move him and frighten him as you would make a locust flee and tremble? **"His majestic snorting is terrible"**—the sneezing of the shadow of his nostrils is a terror, as the Targum translates: "Do you make him tremble like a grasshopper? The sound of the snorting of his nostrils is a terror."

**"He paws in the valley and rejoices in his strength; he goes out to meet the weapons."** <note>Job 39:21</note> The warriors <note>original: *lavakhim*</note> paw in the depth of the plain, perhaps they can capture them for the Kingdom of Heaven. And the horse laughs and rejoices in his strength over the officers <note>original: *saternin*</note> at the awakening of war, amidst the wings of toil and the transformations. **"He goes out to meet the weapons"**—towards the armed men of war, for he desires the magic of the battle-array and what lies beneath. Therefore, he always laughs at war, as the Targum translates: "They dig in the valley and rejoice in their strength, and go out to meet the weapons of war."

**"He mocks at fear and is not dismayed; he does not turn back from the sword."** <note>Job 39:22</note> The explanation is that he scorns every fear of war, and his heart is not shattered because his nature is that of war and might. Therefore, he does not retreat from the sword in battle, as the Targum translates: "He mocks at the struggle and is not broken, and does not return from before the sword."

**"Upon him rattles the quiver, the flashing spear, and the javelin."** <note>Job 39:23</note> **"Upon him is sent the quiver"**—meaning the weapon-case. **"The flashing spear"** is the iron which is on the spear, and the **"javelin"** is a lance, as the Targum translates: "Upon him is cast the weapon, the spear, and the lance."

**"With quaking and rage he swallows the ground; he cannot believe that it is the sound of the shofar."** <note>Job 39:24</note> The explanation is that he moves with agitation and anger, and scatters pits in the earth in order to run from the path of the trenches to fight the enemies. From the great joy the horse has for war, even when he hears the sound of battle, **he cannot believe** that there is a war until he sees the battle with his own eyes. This is the meaning of "he cannot believe that it is the sound of the shofar." And from its strength... why do I need the sound of the shofar? It is to awaken the war, the fighting, the shouting, and the terror from your obligations. And this is what the Targum translates: "In his quaking and rage he makes pits in the earth, and he does not believe any sound of the shofar."

**"At the sound of the shofar he says, 'Aha!' and from afar he smells the battle, the thunder of the captains and the shouting."** <note>Job 39:25</note> The explanation is that whenever he hears the trumpet-blast of war, he prepares himself with joy. From afar he senses the war, the quaking of the captains, and their intoxication with one another. That is to say, even the captains of the walls of the world—he senses the war immediately. This is as the Targum translates: "When the shofar is blown he says, 'I am glad!' and from afar he smells the battle, the shouts of his masters, and the cry of the chargers." The plain meaning of **"from afar he smells the battle"** is that he does not believe the war is happening until he sees it with his own eyes. "Aha!" <note>The Hebrew *He'akh*</note> is interpreted thus.

**"Is it by your wisdom that the hawk soars, spreading its wings toward the south?"** <note>Job 39:26</note> It is hollow like every day when the southern wind and northern wind rest...

<summary>The Ra'avad provides a detailed commentary on the ostrich and the war horse from Job 39, using the Aramaic Targum to emphasize the horse's supernatural bravery and eagerness for battle. The text explores the horse's physical reactions—snorting, pawing, and its inability to "believe" the shofar's call until the fight begins—as a metaphor for strength and divine design.</summary>
<keywords>Job 39, Ra'avad, War Horse, Ostrich, Targum, Shofar, Binah, Strength (Gevurah), Medieval Philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 35 ---
<meta>This page continues the Ra’avad’s commentary on Job 39, moving from the horse and the ostrich to a deeper philosophical and mystical exploration of the differences between human intellect and animal nature. It specifically addresses the concept of "Dveikut" (spiritual attachment) and the symbolic meaning of the "Renanim" (ostrich or wild rooster) in Jewish mystical tradition.</meta>

# Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

Regarding this explanation, know that just as the Master sends the grace of His wisdom into the soul, it must be spread out and prepared for its fellow companions. So too, the Supernal Wisdom <note>Hebrew: *Chokhmah HaElyonah*, referring to the divine source of all knowledge</note> requires a spreading out and a discourse to mend its paths. Knowledge is required to give the matter its proper portion, and this stands as an overseer in the heart and brain, which represent the partial dominion of man—but not in the animals that oppose man. For even if a man were to show them his way and his paths, they would not learn from him, nor understand, nor listen to him, for they are inherently opposed to man. Therefore, a man cannot bind them to himself even with a rope or with thick cords. And even if he wishes to arrange the "depths," which are the depths of wisdom, and influence them through his hand, they will not listen to him because they are oppositional, as it is said: "He will not arrange the depths for the furrow" <note>A mystical reading of Job 39:10</note>, which the Targum <note>The Aramaic translation</note> renders as: "Is it possible that you could tie the wild ox (*re’em*) in the furrows of the plain with his harness, or will he level the valleys after you?"

The "harnessing" is a hint to Wisdom, which is called an "inheritance," as it is said, "Restrain your eyes" <note>Hebrew: *balmei reḥaz einyekha*</note>. Will you tie the wild ox (*re'em*) with his thick cord? Know that whoever serves a specific group of powers, in the same proportion that the soul of the servant relates to the object of service, so in the future path will be the bond (*kesher*) of the servant's soul to the Master. This is the secret of the verse: **"But you who cleave to the Lord your God are all alive today"** <note>Deuteronomy 4:4. The term for "cleaving" is *Dveikut*, a central concept in Jewish mysticism describing attachment to the Divine.</note>. Therefore, whoever serves the Living God and is bound to Him—even if he dies for the sanctification of His Name—behold, the Living God shall give him His strength and His spirit. 

The distinction for one who serves "other gods" is like the parable of the *re’em* <note>Often translated as a wild ox or oryx; sometimes associated with the unicorn in medieval lore</note>, which is drawn from the foundation of the world, as it is said: **"his horns are the horns of the wild ox"** <note>Deuteronomy 33:17</note>. This was said regarding Joseph the Righteous. And these are the meanings of "Will you tie the wild ox with his cord," which refer to possessions and the secret within them—they are "thick cords." Regarding "Will he level the depths for the furrow?"—do not let it enter your mind that a man can prepare for you the "furrow of life" which you desire from this world. Would you trust him because his strength is great, and leave your labor to him? And is it possible that you would trust him to deliver your handiwork, that you should bring in your harvest to him, from which you have been sustained until now? Only if you recognize the Harvester and the Reaper through the service of love can you trust in Him and leave your labor to His kindness (*Hasdo*); if He wills it, He gives, and if He does not will it, He does not give. As the Targum translates: "Is it possible that you will trust in him because his strength is great, and will you leave your labor to him?" 

If so, is it possible that you can rely on him that everything you have sown will be improved, and that all your harvest will be gathered and strengthened by the power of the *re'em*? For he is the "firstborn of the bull," as it is said: "His glory is like the firstling of his bullock, and his horns are like the horns of a wild ox; with them he shall push the people" <note>Deuteronomy 33:17</note>. This is why the Targum translates: "Is it possible that you will trust in him... and leave your labor..." Speaking now of the "face of the eagle."

**"The wings of the ostrich flap joyfully; are they the pinions and plumage of love?"** <note>Job 39:13</note>. This refers to the name of the "Wild Rooster" <note>original: *Tarnegol HaBar*. In Jewish folklore, this is the legendary *Ziz*, a giant bird.</note> in the Book of Job; it is called *Renanim* (Joyful Songs) because it gives forth songs of praise. In the *Heikhalot* literature <term>Heikhalot: Early Jewish mystical texts describing the "palaces" of heaven and the divine chariot</term>, it is written that the "Holy Living Creatures" (*Chayot HaKodesh*) hint at this from within their wings, as it is said: "And I heard the sound of their wings." 

Behold, in this Wild Rooster, whose strength comes from the "face of the eagle," it rises in full joy. And likewise, regarding "the pinions and plumage of the stork," the Sages of blessed memory explained that it was a representation of the intellect, as the Targum renders: "The wing of the wild rooster which praises and shouts, and the wing of the white stork and its feathers." 

**"For she leaves her eggs on the earth and warms them in the dust."** <note>Job 39:14</note>. Because the "wing of songs" contains all types of upper and lower wings—for it includes all the celestial constellations—when she leaves her eggs on the ground and nests them in the dust, the Understanding (*Binah*) warms them from the air without her even touching them. Just by her looking at them, they are warmed, and chicks are born from them. This is what the Targum means: "For she leaves her eggs on the ground and warms them on the earth." **"And forgets that a foot may crush them, or that a wild beast may trample them."** She is hardened against her young, as if they were not hers...

<summary>The Ra’avad interprets Job 39 as a mystical allegory, contrasting the untameable nature of the wild ox and ostrich with the human capacity for "Dveikut" (divine attachment). He links the "Renanim" bird to the celestial "Wild Rooster" of mystical literature, which is said to hatch its eggs through spiritual focus or sight alone.</summary>
<keywords>Job 39, Ra'avad, Dveikut, wild ox (Re'em), Joseph the Righteous, Heikhalot literature, Renanim, Wild Rooster, Binah (Understanding), Targum</keywords>

--- Page 36 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the Ra’avad’s philosophical commentary on Job and the nature of animals into a structured list of the "Thirty-Two Paths of Wisdom." While the previous page discussed the inability of animals to grasp higher wisdom, this page begins to define the specific spiritual "Intelligences" or "Paths" (Netivot) through which divine wisdom emanates into the world. The catchword "The Sixteenth Path" indicates the list continues on the following page.</meta>

# The Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

**The First Path:** is called the **Wondrous Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Mufla*</note>. It is the Primal Intellectual Light and the secret of the First Glory; no created being is able to grasp its true nature.

**The Second Path:** is called the **Illuminating Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Me’ir*</note>. It is the Crown of Creation <note>The *Keter* of the world of *Beriyah*</note> and the radiance of the Equal Unity. It is exalted above every head and is called the "Second Glory" within the tradition—such is its name.

**The Third Path:** is called the **Sanctified Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Mekudash*</note>. It is the foundation of Ancient Wisdom, which is called the "Faith of the Craftsman" <note>A play on words with *Emunat Omen*</note>, and its roots are "Amen." It is the Father of Faith, from which faith emanates.

**The Fourth Path:** is called the **Fixed Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Kavuah*</note>. It is named such because from it all spiritual powers are emanated as subtle spiritualities, which chain down one from another by the power of the Primal Inheritor, may He be blessed.

**The Fifth Path:** is called the **Rooted Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Nishrash*</note>. It is named such because it is the essence of the Equal Unity, and it is uniquely joined to the essence of the "Understanding" <note>Hebrew: *Binah*</note> which is comprised of the boundaries of Ancient Wisdom.

**The Sixth Path:** is called the **Separate Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Nifrad*</note>. It is named such because within it the flow of Emanation <note>Hebrew: *Atzilut*</note> multiplies, and it bestows that flow upon all the blessings which are themselves intertwined.

**The Seventh Path:** is called the **Hidden Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Nistar*</note>. It is named such because it is a brilliant radiance for all the intellectual powers that are seen with the eye of the mind and the thought of faith.

**The Eighth Path:** is called the **Perfect Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Shalem*</note>. It is named such because it is the disposition of Primality, which has no root in which to take hold except in the chambers of Greatness <note>The Sefirah of *Gedulah* or *Chesed*</note> that emanates from the essence of its existence.

**The Ninth Path:** is called the **Pure Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Tahor*</note>. It is named such because it purifies the Sefirot <note>The ten divine emanations or attributes</note>, testing and refining the decree of their form and the substance of their unity, so that they are joined without any cutting or separation.

**The Tenth Path:** is called the **Scintillating Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Mitnotzetz*</note>. It is named such because it ascends and sits upon the Throne of Understanding <note>The Sefirah of *Binah*</note> and shines with the radiance of all the luminaries, bestowing an abundant flow upon the Prince of the Presence <note>Hebrew: *Sar HaPanim*, often identified as the angel Metatron</note>.

**The Eleventh Path:** is called the **Refined Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Metzuchzach*</note>. It is named such because it is the essence of the "Veil" <note>Hebrew: *Pargod*</note> which is arranged within the order of the system; it stands in direct relation before the Cause of Causes.

**The Twelfth Path:** is called the **Lucent Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Bahir*</note>. It is named such because it is the essence of the Great Wheel <note>Hebrew: *Ofan*</note> which is called "Visionary," meaning the place from which the vision of the seers is prophesied in the expanse.

**The Thirteenth Path:** is called the **Conducting Intelligence of Unity** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Manhig HaAchdut*</note>. It is named such because it is the essence of the Glory, and it is the completion of the true nature of the singular spiritual beings.

**The Fourteen Path:** is called the **Illuminating Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Me’ir*</note>. It is named such because it is the essence of the flashing [stone] <note>Hebrew: *mishtahiv*, likely related to the *Chashmal* or flashing brilliance</note> that instructs concerning the mysteries of the holy foundations and their disposition.

**The Fifteenth Path:** is called the **Stabilizing Intelligence** <note>Hebrew: *Sekhel Ma’amid*</note>. It is named such because it stabilizes the essence of creation within the clouds of purity. The masters of contemplation have said of it that it is the "Thick Cloud" <note>Hebrew: *Araphel*</note>, and it is the Cloud in its purity.

<summary>This page lists the first fifteen of the thirty-two mystical "Paths of Wisdom," providing a name and a brief metaphysical definition for each intelligence. These paths describe the progression of divine light from the incomprehensible Primal Light down through the Sefirot and into the structures of the spiritual world.</summary>
<keywords>Thirty-Two Paths of Wisdom, Ra'avad, Intelligence (Sekhel), Emanation (Atzilut), Sefirot, Binah, Glory (Kavod), Metatron, Pargod, Mystical Lights</keywords>

<meta>The previous page concluded the descriptions of the first fifteen "Wondrous Paths of Wisdom." This page provides a deeper philosophical and mystical commentary on the nature of spiritual ascent, using the imagery of birds from the Book of Job, followed by a historical-mythical lineage of how this secret "Kabbalah" (tradition) was transmitted from angels to the Biblical patriarchs.</meta>

## The Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

The law of His action was greater than his own... <note>The text here is fragmentary, likely commenting on the divine nature of creation exceeding human grasp</note>. It is said: "Does the hawk fly by your wisdom and spread its wings toward the south?" <note>Quoting Job 39:26</note>. Our Rabbis of blessed memory explained that his likeness is as the likeness of the "End" <note>Hebrew: *Ketz*, often referring to the end of days or the limit of divine emanation</note>. Look in the Tractate Gittin where the Aramaic translation <note>original: "תרגום" (Targum)</note> explains: "Just as the dove spreads its wings, so does the understanding person spread his wings toward the south." And our colleagues explained the phrase "He shall fly" as a movement of the soul.

He shall consider how the nest is prepared, for the bird circles and moves its wings until it spreads them in total kindness <note>Hebrew: *Chesed*</note>. This refers to the soul rolling and ascending from level to level until it is elevated to the **Attribute of Kindness**. In this way, he even took lessons from the "impure birds" <note>Refers to the birds listed as non-kosher in the Torah, here used as symbols for lower spiritual states</note>, for even they have a cycle of purity through **Understanding** <note>Hebrew: *Binah*</note>. This is the meaning of: "Does the eagle mount up at your command, and make its nest on high?" <note>Job 39:27</note>. This means the soul shall roll upward from the material world until it reaches the glory of its "nest"—which refers to **Beauty** <note>Hebrew: *Tiferet*</note>—and the "young ones" born from it. This is what the Aramaic translation means by "it shall grow strong in its nest and rise to the heights." 

It shall tremble and join itself to the "crag of the rock and the stronghold" <note>Job 39:28</note>. The "rock" is the foundation and the place of dwelling; it means the soul will circle and return to the foundation of the "children of my crag and my stronghold." The "crag of the rock" refers to the Sephirah of **Beauty** <note>Hebrew: *Tiferet*</note>. This reveals to you the ultimate purpose of the soul’s ascent. As the Aramaic says: "It shall dwell and lodge upon the strength of the rock." In the way of the Secret <note>The mystical/Kabbalistic interpretation</note>, "the Lord is my rock and my fortress" <note>Psalm 18:2</note>. 

"From there it spies out the prey; its eyes behold from afar" <note>Job 39:29</note>. This means that from the place of **Beauty** (called "There"), it will "dig" for food to eat—meaning it will gather spiritual sustenance from **Understanding** and **Wisdom**. "From afar" refers to **Wisdom** <note>Hebrew: *Chokhmah*</note>, as it is written: "I said, I will be wise, but it was far from me" <note>Ecclesiastes 7:23</note>. Because of this Wisdom, "its eyes behold"—meaning it gazes and contemplates its source. The Aramaic translation says: "From there it seeks food, its eyes look into the distance." 

"Its young ones suck up blood; and where the slain are, there is it" <note>Job 39:30</note>. This refers to the **Crown** <note>Hebrew: *Keter*</note> at the absolute peak of the ascent, while its "children" are at the absolute limit of the descent. "Its young ones suck up blood" means they are nurtured by the life-force, and in the place of the "slain" (the material realm), there it is found. The Aramaic translation says: "Its young ones swallow blood... where the killed are, there it is." Its likeness is to the **Hidden One** <note>The *Ein Sof* or the highest aspect of the Divine</note>, as they are destined to rise from level to level.

After the attribute of Kindness (*Chesed*), and the children of the Palace... the gates of **Understanding** (*Binah*). Before I present what I received from my teachers, of blessed memory, I have understood that I must write these paths according to the Kabbalah <note>The received tradition</note>, exactly as I received them from the mouths of my teachers.

---

<image-desc>The initial word "מפי" (From the mouth of) is written in a large, bold, square Hebrew script, serving as a decorative opening to the new section.</image-desc>

**From the mouths of the ancient sages** who were occupied with the Way of the True Kabbalah, just as we have mentioned regarding the Thirty-Two Paths of Wisdom which the Sages of the West <note>Usually referring to the scholars of the Land of Israel or North Africa</note> received from the principles of their Torah. Through their judgments and their debates, the laws of their teachers were broadened and became known as the "Laws of the Holy King," may His name be exalted, in order to teach and to add to the ancient Paths of Wisdom.

And this is what they said regarding the transmission of the Kabbalah:
*   The teacher of Adam the first man was: **Raziel** <gloss>(a name meaning "Secret of God")</gloss>
*   The teacher of Shem was: **Jophiel**
*   The teacher of Abraham was: **Zadkiel**
*   The teacher of Isaac was: **Raphael**
*   The teacher of Jacob was: **Peliel**
*   The teacher of Joseph was: **Gabriel** <gloss>(associated with the Sephirah of **Foundation** / *Yesod*)</gloss>
*   The teacher of Moses our Teacher was: **Metatron** <gloss>(associated with the Sephirah of **Kingship** / *Malchut*)</gloss>
*   The teacher of Elijah was: **Maltahiel** <gloss>(associated with the Sephirah of **Kingship** / *Malchut*)</gloss>

Each and every one of these teachers would hand over the tradition to his student, whether in a book or by word of mouth, in order to test him and establish him regarding all that is to come. They would set them up as "Masters of the Tradition" <note>Hebrew: *Ba'alei Kabbalah*</note>. They spoke of the "fountain" of his knowledge, and by the desire of the "Sought-After One" <note>God</note>, he brought forth his name. From that time, he desired the **Thirty-Two Wondrous Paths of Wisdom**. 

The ancients and the masters of ethics called these the "Paths of the Simple," but the ancient sages—the sages of my memory who answered me—behaved according to the Way of the Kabbalah. From the principles of their Torah, their judgments, and their debates, they were called "The Kings." They traveled many roads in order to understand these Paths, which are the "Wondrous Paths of Wisdom" which were explained in their proper order <gloss>(in the Sephirah of **Greatness** / *Gedulah*)</gloss> to Abraham our father, peace be upon him, in the **Tradition of the Covenant**. And these are the paths in their rectified order:

<meta>This page continues the "Introduction of the Ra'avad" (Rabbi Abraham ben David), a commentary on the 32 Paths of Wisdom in Sefer Yetzirah. The previous page used bird imagery from the Book of Job to describe the soul's ascent; this page moves into a systematic philosophical and mystical breakdown of the first three Paths, connecting them to the Sefirot (divine emanations) and medieval concepts of physics and metaphysics.</meta>

# Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

After I have arranged for you the connection of all the thirty-two paths, I saw fit to explain to you a very small amount <note>original: "מעט מזער" (me'at miz'ar) — a biblical idiom for a tiny bit</note> of what we have received concerning their explanation, with the help of God. May He be exalted, the Power of Wisdom, and may He surround us to walk in the way of the Lord and not err in His paths.

Know that the terms (1) Way, (2) Part, (3) Segol, (4) Written, (5) Holam, (6) Melog, and (7) Meteg <note>These terms (א-ז) refer to Hebrew vowels and grammatical accents used here as mystical ciphers for different types of spiritual "paths"</note> are all names assigned to the eternal paths. Because all the engraving of the paths was done by engraving into an **opaque sphere** <note>A concept in early Kabbalah describing the undifferentiated state of the universe before the emergence of light and form</note> in the likeness of standing sand, which is the "matter" for all seals; thus all the letters and paths are explored. The end of all things is in the seal of its breadth and its depth, hidden from all yet the "Voice" in all; "the whole earth is full of His glory" <note>Isaiah 6:3</note>. The *Chayyah* <note>A high-ranking angel or "living creature" from Ezekiel's vision</note> is the source of His glory... and because the engravings indicate to us the paths to draw near to the ultimate goal, it is appropriate to investigate them. Therefore, I saw fit to explain a tiny bit of what I received in the explanation of these aforementioned "writings."

**The First Writing** is called "Assured" <note>Hebrew: *Muvtach*</note>. Its explanation is from the language of "He cleaves" <note>original: "יפלח" (yiflach), likely referring to Job 16:13</note>, according to the Aramaic translation. There is no one as wise in our heart to say that anything hidden is covered and concealed from everything else. It is like a shadow that is not grasped by anything besides itself; this is the **Hidden Intellect** <note>original: *Sekhel HaMufla* — associated with Keter, the highest and most unknowable divine emanation</note> in the world of our intellection. It is the "Primordial Glory," for it is known that the form of the letter *Aleph* <note>The text uses "ה"ף", likely a scribal shorthand or OCR error for "אלף"</note> is the form of all letters, and all paths are sealed within it. Therefore, it is called the "Assured Writing" because it does not depart from its level or its type. It is called **Primordial** because it precedes all levels and all existing things. No creature can understand its existence, just as we cannot understand the essence of the "Cause of Causes" <note>A philosophical term for God as the ultimate source of all existence</note>.

**The Second Path** is called the "Illuminating Intellect" <note>original: *Sekhel HaMazhir*</note>. It is the **Crown of Creation** <note>original: *Keter HaBeriah*</note> and the "Simple Eternity." Know that the term "cleaving" <unclear>OCR: כע'</unclear> does not apply to a fixed point, neither a lower point nor a concentrated point. For "matter" indicates that which is not grasped by its placement. Certainly, the "cleaving" and the matter of "eternity" and "concentration" refer to the Cause of Causes; for the "Eternity" intended here is not a "subject" <term>Accident (Mikreh): In medieval philosophy, a property that can change without changing the essence of the thing (like color), as opposed to "substance"</term>. It is within the category of quantity, both discrete and continuous... and the Writing called "cleaving" has no quantity, for nothing "hidden" can receive quantity, let alone the Cause of Causes. This is the **Illuminating Intellect**, meaning that the eyes of all beings are darkened from looking at the "Hidden Intellect" (the First Path), but in this Second Intellect, there is some small degree of attainment of the Blessed One. Therefore, it is called "Illuminating." It is further called "Crown of Creation" because it has already been explained that the senses cannot grasp a created thing purely by chance, but rather grasp a specific quantity... and it certainly cannot grasp a "hidden" accident. It is called "Simple Eternity" because it is the recipient of the "One Unity," and thus all beings declare here "I have been gracious to whom I will be gracious" <note>Exodus 33:19</note>, even if they are not worthy. It is called the "Face of Glory" and this "Writing" is called the **Attribute of Loving-kindness** <note>Hebrew: *Midat HaChasidut* or *Chesed*</note>.

**The Third Path** is called the "Sanctified Intellect" <note>original: *Sekhel HaMukdash*</note>. It is primordial to all types of primordiality and is prepared to receive the form of **Understanding** <note>Hebrew: *Binah*</note> in a way that it is "heated" to receive the form. It is the **Foundation** <note>Hebrew: *Yesod*</note> and the "Head of Wisdom," for from it the outcomes of Wisdom go out to all the Sefirot. For it is the **Primordial Wisdom**... and the "Flowing Wisdom" is called "seasoned with time." May it be His will... <unclear>fragments regarding "ten fingers"</unclear>.

<meta>The previous page introduced the mystical "32 Paths of Wisdom" from Sefer Yetzirah (The Book of Formation), explaining the first fifteen paths as levels of divine emanation. This page continues the sequence from the 16th to the 32nd path, detailing how these spiritual "Intelligences" govern everything from the souls of the pious to the movement of the planets and the nature of physical matter.</meta>

# Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

| | |
|----------|----------|
| **The 16th Path** | It is called the **Eternal Intelligence**. It is named so because it is the delight of the Glory <note>original: "כבוד" (Kavod) — a term for the Divine Presence or the highest spiritual light</note>, such that there is no glory lower than it in its essence. The power of this Glory is prepared in the Garden of Eden, which is set aside for the pious. |
| **The 17th Path** | It is called the **Sensing Intelligence**. This power is prepared for the pious to strengthen them, so they may clothe themselves in the Holy Spirit. It is the power of the Crown <note>original: "כתר" (Keter) — the highest of the Sefirot</note> and the foundation of transformation, matching the standing of the heavenly beings. |
| **The 18th Path** | It is called the **Intelligence of the House of Abundance**. Through the investigation of this path, one draws out hidden mysteries that shimmer within our hearts; by cleaving to the investigation of its thought, one reaches the highest level. |
| **The 19th Path** | It is called the **Intelligence of the Secret of all Spiritual Activities**. It is so named because of the abundance that spreads through it from the Supernal Blessing and the exalted Glory. |
| **The 20th Path** | It is called the **Intelligence of the Will**. It is named so because it is the fundamental structure of all created beings. Through this Intelligence, one can know the entire reality of the Ancient Wisdom <note>original: "חכמה הקדומה" (Chokhmah ha-Kedumah)</note>. |
| **The 21st Path** | It is called the **Intelligence of the Desired Aim**. It is named so because it receives the divine flow of abundance in order to bestow its blessing upon all existing things. |
| **The 22nd Path** | It is called the **Faithful Intelligence**. It is named so because within it, spiritual powers increase so that they may be close to all those who are being initiated <note>original: "מתחנכים" — those training or being educated in spiritual mysteries</note> within the celestial sphere. |
| **The 23rd Path** | It is called the **Sustaining Intelligence**. It is named so because it is the power of existence and endurance for all the *Sefirot* <note>The ten divine attributes or emanations in Kabbalah</note>. |
| **The 24th Path** | It is called the **Imaginative Intelligence**. It is named so because it gives form to all the likenesses and visions created in the "imaginations" fitting for the prophets. |
| **The 25th Path** | It is called the **Establishing Intelligence**. It is named so because it is the "Ancient Trial" through which the Creator, may He be blessed, tests all the pious. |
| **The 26th Path** | It is called the **Renewing Intelligence**. It is named so because through it, the Holy One, Blessed be He, creates something new for all the holy ones who are renewed in the creation of the world. |
| **The 27th Path** | It is called the **Palpable Intelligence**. It is named so because from it was created the intellect of every creature beneath the highest sphere, as well as their sensations. |
| **The 28th Path** | It is called the **Natural Intelligence**. It is named so because through it, the nature of every being beneath the sphere of the Sun is brought to its total perfection. |
| **The 29th Path** | It is called the **Guided Intelligence**. It is named so because it describes every physical body <note>original: "גרם" (gerem) — a celestial or physical body</note> that is guided under the structure of all the spheres during their growth. |
| **The 30th Path** | It is called the **Universal Intelligence**. It is named so because within it, the stargazers and astrologers <note>original: "חוזי הכוכבים והמזלות" — literally "seers of stars and zodiac signs"</note> include their associations, their cycles, and their knowledge of the reversals of their orbits. |
| **The 31st Path** | It is called the **Constant Intelligence**. Why is it called so? Because it guides the path of the Sun and the Moon according to their nature, each one in the sphere appropriate for it. |
| **The 32nd Path** | It is called the **Served Intelligence**. It is named so because it is prepared for all those who make use of the service of the seven wandering stars <note>The seven classical planets: Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn</note> in their movements. |

->(And this is the end of the explanation of the thirty-two paths in brief language.)<-

<meta>Translator Note: There appears to be a numbering overlap or discrepancy between the previous page summary provided and the current source text. While the previous page reportedly reached Path 20, the current Hebrew manuscript page begins with the 8th Path. I am translating the Hebrew text exactly as it appears on this page (Paths 8 through 17) to ensure accuracy to the physical manuscript.</meta>

# Introduction of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

The **8th Path** is called the **Perfect and Prepared Intelligence** <note>original: "שכל שלם ומוכן" (Sekhel Shalem u-Mukhan)</note> because they <note>the preceding emanations</note> are completed and have reached their end through it. Since the paths are finished and completed, and it is impossible to understand the purpose of this path except through a "shining intelligence," it is therefore called "Perfect." Do not be surprised by this, for this is the separation between every demonstrated intelligence. This is a secret: for the **Supernal Path** (the Crown) <note>original: "עט'" abbreviation for "Atarah" (Crown), referring to the Sefirah of Malchut or the highest point of a sequence</note> cannot be understood as to whether it belongs to the realm of thought or the realm of speech, for it is hidden and concealed and does not change its place; it is like a stake driven into the earth. Anyone who seeks to know the truth of this path must investigate it through the "investigations of Greatness" (Splendor) <note>original: "התפארת" (Tiferet) — the Sefirah of balance and beauty</note> that is emanated from it.

The **9th Path** is called the **Pure Intelligence** <note>original: "שכל טהור" (Sekhel Tahor)</note>. It is called so because it purifies and cleanses the decree of the **Sefirot** <note>term: Sefirot (Divine emanations or channels through which God interacts with the universe)</note> from obstruction and the excess of damage. It is pure and refined against the "Transformations." Thus, it cleanses, triumphs, and purifies the decree of their structure, removing the Transformations from them. Because it prepares the "unions" <note>original: "הלחמות" (conjunctions/fusions)</note> in order to unify without cutting or separation.

The **10th Path** is called the **Scintillating Intelligence** <note>original: "שכל מתנוצץ" (Sekhel Mitnotzetz)</note>. It is called so because it rises from a transcendent intelligence and sits upon the **Throne of Understanding** <note>original: "כיסא הבינה" (Kisse ha-Binah) — referring to the third Sefirah, Binah</note>, the Supernal Throne. It projects its light toward Splendor and the Crown, and toward all the lights, and it pours out the abundance of its blessing to the **Prince of the Face** <note>term: Prince of the Face (Sar ha-Panim, a high-ranking archangel, often identified as Metatron, who stands in the immediate presence of God)</note>. It is called "Scintillating" after the recipient who mediates the glow from Understanding.

The **11th Path** is called the **Polished Intelligence** <note>original: "שכל מצוחצח" (Sekhel Metzukhtzakh)</note>. It is called so because it is the "Branch of the Veil" and the "Curtain" <note>original: "פרגוד" (Pargod) and "פרכת" (Parokhet)</note> arranged in the order of the system of the upper paths and the lower paths. The relationship of the paths stands before the **Cause of Causes**, as it is said: "The Blessed Greatness is for you, between the Holy and the Holy of Holies." It does not always enter the Holy from within the Veil of the *Pargod*, like the matter of the [letter] Resh. Opposite the Veil was the "Heavy Light," acting as the separator between the "Vessels of the Soul" and the "Vessels of the Garment."

The **12th Path** is called the **Luminous Intelligence** <note>original: "שכל בהיר" (Sekhel Bahir)</note>. It is called so because it is the "Distance" that every vision shows to those who **Descend the Chariot** <note>term: Descenders of the Chariot (Yordei Merkavah, early Jewish mystics who sought visions of the Divine Throne-Chariot)</note>, and the seers grasp it. It is called the "Wheel of Greatness" because within that distance, upper and lower elements are blended, as are all hidden things. This is the secret of "Bright light in the skies" <note>Job 37:21</note>. It is also called *Middot* <note>measures/attributes</note> after the concept of "Measured Deaths" <note>original: "ממות מדידות"</note>.

The **13th Path** is called the **Intelligence that Directs all Living Beings** <note>original: "שכל מנהיג כל החיות"</note>. It is the branch of the Glory which receives from all the paths. It completes and adds truth to the **Cherubim** <note>term: Cherubim (Angelic beings who, in mystical literature, support the Divine Throne)</note> who investigate, who are established in the Supernal Unity. Everything that is established is established through this 13th path; therefore, the word **One** <note>original: "אחד" (Echad)</note> has the numerical value of 13.

The **14th Path** is called the **Illuminating Intelligence** <note>original: "שכל המאיר"</note>. It is called so because it points to the mysteries and secrets of the Most High for all who are set apart from it. Its matter concerns the secrets of the religion. It is the branch of the **Chashmal** <note>term: Chashmal (a mystical "glowing amber" or "speaking silence" seen in Ezekiel’s vision of the Chariot)</note> (the Crown), in whose hand are all the supernal secrets. It reveals the secret of the Sanctuary and its structure.

The **15th Path** is called the **Stabilizing Intelligence** <note>original: "שכל מעמיד"</note>. It is called so because it stabilizes the "Branch of Blessing" (the Crown) in its "Thick Darkness of Purity." This path brings the Thick Darkness <note>original: "ערפל" (Araphel) — the dark cloud where God's presence dwells</note> close and stabilizes it so that it is fit to receive the encompassing powers of the Supernal Might from all created things. Were it not for the strengthening of the Thick Darkness through this path, the power of the Darkness would not have the strength to receive the form of the created beings. Concerning this, it is said: "When I made the cloud its garment, and thick darkness its swaddling band" <note>Job 38:9</note>.

The **16th Path** is called the **Eternal Intelligence** <note>original: "שכל נצחי" (Sekhel Nitzchi)</note> because it is prepared for anyone who conquers their inclination and its "Transformation." It is a branch of the Glory (the Crown). In this path, the Crown is included as a delight and refinement for the commandments of the Lord. It is called the Garden of Eden, prepared for the pious.

The **17th Path** is called the **Sensing Intelligence** <note>original: "שכל המרגש"</note>. Know that by the power of **Splendor** (*Tiferet*), all the "Mights" are drawn into the heavens above, and upon, and below...
