### The Left Column: On the Anatomy of the Nerves
<note>This column provides a medieval anatomical mapping, correlating the "32 Paths of Wisdom" from the Sefer Yetzirah to the human nervous system. Here, the author identifies 31 nerves/paths stemming from the spine.</note>

And know that from the **spinal cord** <term>Chut ha-Shidrah</term> which extends from
the brain, there spread out from them **31 paths** <note>In medieval anatomy, these represent the nerves</note>, and all of them
are **doubled** <note>pairs</note> except for the one path that has
no pair; and they are 32 paths <note>The text says 31, but implies 32 total paths of wisdom by including the brain or the single path</note> that extend from the 
spinal cord.

**The 1st Path** extends from the nape,
which is between the vertebrae of the neck,
and it ascends until it winds around the head.
**The 2nd Path** extends from the nape, which is between
the 1st and 2nd vertebrae, and it ascends until it winds
in the skin of the head and provides it with the sensory nerve, and it also 
reaches the front of the eyes and the face,
and it provides them with movement.
**The 3rd Path** flows from the nape, which is between the 2nd
and 3rd vertebrae, and it ascends to the eye and the face on one side and gives
them sense and movement.
**The 4th Path** flows
from the nape, which is between the 3rd and 4th vertebrae, until
it winds to the ear and above it.
**The 5th Path** flows from the nape, which is between the 4th
and 5th vertebrae, and from it, it reaches the [sense of] touch; part of it is
the cause for the movement of the head and part of it to the shoulder.
**The 6th Path** flows from the nape, which is between the 
5th and 6th vertebrae; and **The 7th [Path]** flows from the nape
which is between the 6th and 7th vertebrae, and they ascend—
these to the head and part of them to the spine
and the [sense of] touch.
**The 8th Path** flows from the nape,
which is between the 7th and 8th vertebrae, and some of them ascend
to the upper arm and its underside, and some to
the shoulder.
**The 9th Path** connects with the 
6th path at the shoulder, and they bring the [power of the] brain to the upper
arm.
**The 10th Path** descends to the arm, and in it is
the movement of the arm, and part of it brings the brain <note>the nerve impulse</note> to the joint
of the arm, and part of the 10th path gives the brain
and reaches the shoulder.
**The 11th Path** <note>OCR says 10th again, but it follows the 10th</note> flows between
the 9th and 10th vertebrae, and this path spreads out
through the half of the body called the lungs, and part of it through the ways
of the spine, and part of it descends to the stomach and the [power of]
the Law <note>likely a typo or specific term for 'function'</note> reaches it...

### [Right Column]

And because of the liver. And it has thirty rows, hinting at the heart and the mind <note>original: 'לבי ולשכיי'; 'Sekhvi' is a biblical term for the intellect or the heart's inner chambers</note> which distinguish between the Holy and the Holy of Holies. And there is an appointed power that distinguishes between the first writing and the last writing. Behold, within the thirty "writings" <note>likely referring to nerve pathways or divine inscriptions within the body</note> there are seventy tendons, and among them are veins and pulsing arteries, and tendons that are connected and pulsing. 

And some of them are organs, and some of them are "writings" for the sake of vision, and some of them are "preservatives" <note>original: 'סמותרי'; likely a corruption of a Greek-derived medical term for stabilizers</note> as the naturalists <note>medieval scientists</note> have said: "The best of the cord returns to time, and the best of the breath returns to the foundation, and the best of the flame returns to the source." And evil is placed by the power of the taker upon the "house of the seeds" <note>likely referring to the reproductive organs or the lower abdomen</note>. 

And a similar example of this exists in the universe: the "good" within the soul ascends to the **Sefirot** <note>The ten divine emanations in Kabbalah</note>, while the "digestion" <note>the processing of physical waste</note> is cast down to the "bound ones" <note>lower forces or shells</note>. This is sufficient for the one who understands.

And those "writings" are all from the hidden and concealed Source, which no created being can truly comprehend, and no thought can grasp its existence. We have included it in our senses, and it is the "expanses of the tongue." As it is written: *If a matter is too wondrous for you* <note>Deuteronomy 17:8</note>, the Aramaic translation is: "If a matter is hidden."

The revealed part is called **Aleph**, and its covering is **Lamed**, because it is hidden from our comprehension. There is a "book," a "name," and a "distinction," because there is a difference between the **Attribute of Justice** and the **Attribute of Goodness**. For the "tongue" <note>original: 'לשון'</note> represents the lack and the distance, and therefore it has a small point, and it is like a mature girl to a young one. 

And the secret of the "Cup of Glory" <note>margin: the covering of the Throne</note> <note>margin: the Splendor</note> is the simple unity, which is the true upright Goodness between the righteous and the wicked in His forgiveness. And the secret of "extracting that which is outside" <note>referring to speech or creative emission</note> is the secret of our language being "acquired" <note>referring to the conventional vs. natural nature of language</note>. 

Because of this, He is called "Distinguished from all." And all the distinguished beings and the wise ones originate from the "Wellspring of Wisdom." They all exist through Mercy and through the "engravings" <note>original: 'הקקיס/חקוקים'</note> in **Wisdom** first <note>margin: He looked</note>, because Wisdom includes all the expansions and the definitions. Afterward, all the specific details of the "engravings" were marked in Wisdom, and the beginning was in Wisdom through a single existence, and its name is **Y-K** <note>The first two letters of the Tetragrammaton, often associated with Wisdom and Understanding</note>. Through it, all existences called "the thirty-two paths" came into being.

### [Left Column]

And the foundation upon us is the matter of our thought in the perfection of its continuity. And perhaps you will learn—has it not already been explained that everything that comes into being must perish? <note>A fundamental Aristotelian principle: 'Hovah ve-Hefsed' (Generation and Corruption)</note>. And if it was small at the beginning and then perished, know that it has a persistence that does not come from its own wisdom but from the center where it dwells, according to the laws of natural things which are necessitated by the natural order. 

For that which comes into being according to natural law will persist in its standing by necessity, following the natural custom. For just as its nature required that it not exist and then afterward it did exist, so too its nature requires that it shall not exist in eternity forever. It has already been explained that this "existence" does not complete the obligation toward it according to its nature, but rather according to our Torah-based claim, which is that the existence of things and their destruction depends on His Will, blessed be He. 

According to this view, the Blessed One, in the Kindness that preceded all, placed it in "rest" <note>original: 'טיפסר המנוח'; likely a corruption of a philosophical term for the 'state of rest'</note>. This rest is a necessity for the whole matter, as a portion of His Will. If He wishes, it will exist, and if He wishes, He will nullify it. And if He wishes, He will return it by the decree of His Wisdom. He held it fast in the "waters" <note>likely referring to the primordial elements</note> and made it persist just as He persists, blessed be He.

And the master, **Rabbeinu Bahya** <note>Bahya ben Asher, 13th-century commentator</note>, of blessed memory, wrote in the book *The Foundation of the Faith*: "It is right for one to say that he fears the collapse of the structure, for the properties of the great things that come into being are according to their inhabitants. For the material which is in the dust will fall to destruction from their path. So too, for the Blessed Name, for He is the Source found as Good in Kindness. He is Good, and the beginning is Good."

And even though things perish, the ultimate "Goodness" is not prevented. For the Good is brought near for the sake of the slight Evil <note>referring to the philosophical idea that local 'evil' or decay serves a universal 'good'</note>. And the "absence" <note>non-existence</note> is not like the quality of existence in its totality, but rather in its parts. For when a person is born, he is a "possessor of grace," but if a part of his parts is destroyed, he will not be nourished and will not stand. So too, the destruction of a part is considered like the death of the whole. And this is what is said...

<meta>catchword: ...to his detriment</meta>

### -> Maimonides, of Blessed Memory, Chapter 1: The Explanation Thereof <-

**i 7 b 1**

Existence is worthy to be called the purpose of all, the beginning and the head of all beginnings. We could not [comprehend] the world, and within the boundaries of folly, we shall not understand it. He prepared in His wisdom the nature of the heavens. (Until here).

If our adversary and opponent should argue against us, saying: "We have investigated the source of his abandonment of the faith in the *Eternity of the World* <note>The philosophical belief, championed by Aristotle, that the universe had no beginning and will have no end.</note> in the book called *Natural Hearing* <note>original: 'שמע טבעי'; the standard Hebrew title for Aristotle’s *Physics* (Greek: *Physica Auscultatio*).</note>." For the world as a whole never was [created] nor fell into non-existence. He [Aristotle] chose to discuss motion, and said in the eighth part <note>Book VIII of Aristotle's *Physics*.</note> of his investigation: "Is it possible for all motion to be renewed <note>i.e., to have a beginning</note> after not having existed?" And the commentators explained that in his investigation of all motion, he aimed at everything that is defined by motion, whether in truth, by agreement, or by force. He clarified that **Time** is eternal, just as it is revealed that when a "year" is renewed, it is created, and everything created exists within time; if so, time is created within a time which existed previously. Furthermore, **Potentiality** <note>original: 'כח'; the capacity for something to happen.</note> precedes **Actuality** <note>original: 'פועל'; the realization of that capacity.</note> in time; therefore, before its creation, it existed in potentiality, and this potentiality requires a subject. And since all motion exists only in time, it must [have existed] from the beginning of its rest.

We shall answer their danger: Know that there is no **decisive proof** <note>original: 'מופת חותך'; a logical demonstration that provides absolute certainty.</note> here to destroy us, and this does not contradict the secrets of our wisdom. Our preparation is like the nature of the Prophet <note>implying divine revelation as a source of knowledge superior to logic</note> and as has already been mentioned. Regarding Aristotle and this judgment concerning the matter of Time, all the natural philosophers have already agreed upon it. And this was the view of **Plato**; and in speaking of Plato <note>original: 'פליטו'</note>, he said that all the natural philosophers who preceded him believed that motion eventually perishes—that it is born and destroyed. However, according to Plato and others, motion was perishable, and likewise, time also passes away and is destroyed. And if this question—namely, the Eternity of the World—is clarified by him through proof, why did he [Aristotle] need to bring evidence against the opinion of Plato? Since you know that because of this, the proof is not complete, and one should not hope for it to be settled as if all the sages were in agreement.

<column-break/>

...a palace <note>original: 'פלטין'; from the Latin *palatium*.</note>, and in that height which is spirit according to... the Supreme One in His light, rivers break through, and "a precious vessel his eye saw" <note>A play on Job 28:10: "His eye sees every precious thing."</note>. For in the absence of the investigation of Wisdom, like a golem <note>an unformed matter or substance</note> to the Creator who encompasses all true existents, are the **Forms of the Letters**, which are the letters of the Torah. They are the secrets of the Place <note>term: <term>The Place (HaMakom)</term>: a traditional Jewish name for God, implying He is the "place" where the world exists, rather than the world being His place.</note>, missing from our lack of the **Pen of Light**, which is the rock (for) there are the letters. And the kings in the words of their engraving are called "Paths." And behold, there are **32** <note>original: 'לב''; the number 32 is central to *Sefer Yetzirah*, representing the 32 Paths of Wisdom.</note> divisions of the letters, as I have explained to you from here and there. And behold, every single letter has all the other letters engraved within it, and according to this, **Aleph** <note>The first letter of the Hebrew alphabet.</note> alternates with all of them, and all of them with Aleph. And from those paths, they all flowed... and natures, and a multitude of types of lights... in the lamps, and rivers break through, and "every precious thing his eye saw," as is the number of the illuminations in the *Book of Speech* <note>original: 'ספר המלל'</note>.

And there are ten lights <note>referring to the ten *Sefirot*, the emanations of God in Kabbalah</note>, and behold, my illuminations within them were hinted at and found as the likeness of the Height of the Throne. For according to the name established for the dwelling place of the Sages, "He reveals deep things from the darkness and brings out the shadow of death to light" <note>Job 12:22</note>. And behold, everything is before me, and in His light, the deep flower [blossoms]. And because I have passed through and examined all the hidden things... to the light. These are the secrets of Knowledge and Wisdom.

**"But where shall Wisdom be found?"** <note>Job 28:12</note> This means: from the **Ayin** <note>original: 'אין'; literally "Nothingness," referring to the Infinite (Ein Sof) which is beyond human comprehension.</note> that is not perceived by our senses. From there it was inherited, and it inherits "Yesh" <note>original: 'י"ס'; referring to 'Yesh' (Somethingness/Being) or potentially the 10 Sefirot.</note> from the Living One. However, this is not a "Total Absence" <note>original: 'העדר גמור'; a philosophical term for absolute nothingness.</note>, for a living thing does not come into being from a random thing... even more so, Being (*Yesh*) does not emerge from Nothing (*Ayin*) except by way of the "Primeval Ayin." Since it is not perceived—neither from the side of its cause, nor from its own side—its Cause is the **Cause of Causes** <note>original: 'עלת העלות'; the First Cause or God.</note>. And it is called the "Ancient Living One" because it preceded the world. And this is not a physical thing, and this is the "Place of Understanding." These are Knowledge and Wisdom, the cause of the dwelling of the Divine Presence (*Shekhinah*). For the letter *Gimel* <note>Numerical value 3.</note> hints to them, and *Yod-He* <note>A name of God.</note> to Wisdom. **"Man knows not the price thereof"** <note>Job 28:13</note>; meaning, man has no power to investigate and know the value of Wisdom and how its worth was established.

<meta>Catchword: from his fruit (מפרונירפ - likely a corruption of 'מפריו')</meta>

<summary>This page explores the tension between Aristotelian proofs for the eternity of the universe and the Kabbalistic view of creation from "Ayin" (Divine Nothingness). It transitions from a logical critique of Aristotle and Plato to a mystical explanation of the 32 Paths of Wisdom and the letters of the Torah as the building blocks of reality.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the philosophical and mystical inquiry into the nature of the universe. While the previous page touched upon the physical and spiritual structure of the body, this page delves into the cosmological foundations found in "Sefer Yetzirah" (The Book of Creation) and the debate between Aristotelian philosophy and Jewish theology regarding whether the world is eternal or created in time.</meta>

# Raavad, of Blessed Memory, Chapter 1: Maimonides, of Blessed Memory

**i 7 b 2**

And if these were according to matter, for their words are also the general principles of existence, etc. To share the virtue of the Congregation of Israel, which is called, etc. And powers—and the explanation is: by the power of this vitality, by the power of our portion, etc. For when all the clear visions and all the looking-glasses <note>original: "הספקלריאות" (Aspaklaria); a term used in the Talmud and Kabbalah to describe a prophetic lens or mirror through which a prophet perceives divine truth.</note> and all the prophecies in the manner of those seen in prophecy as through a looking-glass, and in the investigation, etc.

He reached the principle of the clarity of existence, etc. Matter in three "books" <note>A reference to the foundational concept in *Sefer Yetzirah* that the world was created through three "Sefarim": Sefer (Book/Scribe), Sfar (Number), and Sippur (Story/Communication).</note> which he began to explain from within it, etc. All the upper existences, and he included them in three books, etc. You should know that the *Sefirot* <note>The ten divine emanations or attributes in Kabbalah through which the Infinite interacts with the finite world.</note> are called "Books," etc.

Derived from the root of "frost" <note>original: "כפור" (Kefor); a mystical play on words connecting the Hebrew for "frost" with the "sapphire" clarity of the heavenly spheres.</note>, and he said that through it... the three elements... the paths of all prophecies in "Book" and "Book," etc. And "Story" <note>original: "ספור" (Sippur).</note> is explained as the three letters... which are Yod, Hey, and Vav, and including the final Hey... <note>These are the four letters of the Tetragrammaton, the essential Name of God.</note> For it was caused to be born as we are... as mentioned in this writing, there are three types of script, etc. And names and books: "Book," "Number," and "Story."

And he said: for the form of all beings is drawn from the Blessed One, from the source of the Activators; it flows continuously until a known end. And out of the motion, the beginning began to produce heat, and from heat, a flame, and from a flame, a spark, and from the spark, fire. And behold, the fire was drawn out and rolled upon the sphere in its circle, and from the circle, chaos <note>original: "תהו" (Tohu).</note> was formed to the water. The Holy One, Blessed be He, blew upon the waters, and through it the waters were heated, and they began to be... for the heavens shall not be without rain. And all your deeds shall be for the sake of Heaven.

As it is said: He wraps Himself in light as with a garment, stretching out the heavens like a curtain <note>A reference to Psalm 104:2, describing God's act of creation as a physical manifestation of light.</note>. And behold, he was precise regarding the *Sefirot* of the heavens within it, and the engravings and depictions for all the general created beings. And they are like frozen things... and the heavens are a firmament like the "awesome ice" <note>A reference to Ezekiel 1:22, describing the celestial chariot.</note>. They are sealed in the likeness of a warm hand, and it is revealed without power; and this spirit... wonders.

<column-break/>

According to Nachmanides <note>original: "רמב"ן" (Rabbi Moses ben Nahman).</note>, for every existing thing is subject to destruction and every opening has a width, for the seed of the opening... its motion and its cessation, its affliction and a little fear. And it turns into the form of that which is coming into being until it is subdued and receives the absolute form of the Cold Intelligence, as mentioned by Rabbi Jacob son of Rehob. So it is in the book called *Heichalot* <note>original: "היכלות" (Palaces); a genre of early Jewish mystical literature detailing the ascent to the divine throne.</note>.

Those who know claim regarding the manner of investigation, in the way that breaks through from the absolute world to the rest, and destruction. For anything that has a beginning has an end; and as for matter, its matter is the investigation. A thing that has an end and a beginning is without doubt [finite]. But for a thing that had the beginning of the end, it was possible for it... the absolute simple speculation shall not be absent, nor was it hidden from the judgment of natural laws.

The investigation proved that the ancient heavens and the ancient earth... may He, blessed be He, renew the good... the sons of man, blessed, and they shall be many. And He shall increase [the fruit of] the earth, for they are parts of everything. So it is expanded upon in the *Book of Heichalot*, and it says that it is the language of "Bara" <note>original: "ברא"; the Hebrew verb for "Created," specifically used for creation out of nothing (ex nihilo).</note>, and it is the beginning from the lack of necessity and its power... as the Holy Torah says: "Created." *God created the heavens and the earth.*

Moses said this to clarify this inquiry against Aristotle <note>original: "לרסטו" (Aristo); the text challenges the Aristotelian view of the eternity of the world.</note> who thought the world was simple... for behold, it is written in the *Book of the Apple* <note>original: "ספר התפוח" (Sefer HaTappuach); a medieval work translated into Hebrew and attributed to Aristotle, often used by Jewish thinkers to harmonize philosophy with faith.</note> of Maimonides: the engravings and depictions are of benefit to these powers. Its action on the higher world will teach the philosophers, and from it, one will know the intention and power. For every effect has a cause <note>Literally, "for every acted-upon there is an actor."</note>, until he reaches, through his wisdom, the First Cause <note>original: "לפועל הראשון" (The First Agent/Actor); a philosophical term for God as the primary source of all movement and existence.</note> from His capacity.

<summary>This page explores the mystical creation of the universe through the "three books" of Sefer Yetzirah and defends the theological concept of Creation ex Nihilo (creation from nothing) against Aristotle's theory of an eternal universe. It cites Nachmanides and the pseudo-Aristotelian "Book of the Apple" to argue that investigating the natural world leads the philosopher to the "First Cause."</summary>
<keywords>Sefer Yetzirah, Sefirot, Aspaklaria, Creatio ex Nihilo, Aristotle, Nachmanides, Maimonides, First Cause, Book of the Apple, Heichalot, Cosmology</keywords>

# ## RAMBAM, of Blessed Memory

It is explained in the Arabic language as <term>halkad sahal</term> <note>original: "חלקאד סהל"; likely an Arabic phrase meaning "easy creation" or a linguistic classification</note> by Menachem, 
and it is strong; and under this, everything will be like that which is not 
established from their midst. We have finished with it, therefore "balance <term>Shaddai</term>" <note>original: "שדי" (Almighty); here being discussed in a grammatical or structural context</note>
order, and not a mere attachment from the order of the celestial systems. And it howls 
in hunger, and he explains "he shall chastise" from "death," and "save me from the hand 
of death." We have an established rule: there is a version from the "Version of Life" before 
which the good is known. Therefore, the Master, Rabbi Hasdai <note>Rabbi Hasdai Crescas (c. 1340–1410), a major philosopher who critiqued Aristotelianism.</note> 
corrected it; and the Babylonian version explained it as "the Living God" who is 
the God of Israel, as it is said: "the voice of the living God." 
And there is a version that counts "Life which is 
Shaddai," and this would be an exaggeration in contemplation, according to the commentary of Rabbi Samuel <note>Samuel ibn Tibbon (c. 1165–1232), the famous translator of Maimonides’ Guide for the Perplexed.</note> 
who explained "Shaddai" as literal fire, not... that the "Shin" <note>The Hebrew letter 'ש'</note> 
stops, and after the portion of... and Meshullam the Levite, of blessed memory, 
expounded "Life." However, I say according to 
the way of the plain meaning that the first version is 
more correct, for we do not find in any place 
that it says "Shaddai in the fire of life," but rather "the Living God." 
The heart of the essence <term>Qualio</term> <note>original: "קאליו"; likely a transliteration of the Latin/Aristotelian term "Qualitas" (quality) or an Arabic equivalent</note> is not a body nor a force 
within a body; if so, it cannot receive death. And it remains, for the body of 
the essence <term>Qualio</term> is exalted and uplifted above them. 
And if we say "Living," it is not our own vitality 
that is our intention, for we have never understood "Life" 
except as biological life. And if so, our intention is to say 
"Living God" in contrast to the description of the gods of the nations 
who are dead. This is in order to negate from Him the opposite, 
which is death, not to affirm for Him a "life" in the way that our mind 
understands; for it is not in our mind to understand life 
except as motion and movement. And it has already been clarified 
in the Holy Philosophy <note>A term for Aristotelian philosophy as synthesized with Torah.</note> that He, may He be blessed, 
is exalted above these things. However, our Sages say 
of Him that He is "Living" to negate from Him the description of death 
and likeness. In this same way, one says "Kindness" to negate 
from Him its opposite; not to affirm for Him a mode of vitality 
like our own. And understand this: for He, may He be blessed, is not a soul, nor 
does He descend into an essence; He is not a mode that can be divided, nor a mode 
that can be attached.

precious and... The Targum says: "We shall not arrange 
another wealth like gold and glass, if one did not give

<summary>This page discusses the theological definition of God as "Living," arguing that it is a negative attribute meant to exclude the possibility of death rather than to describe biological life. It also provides a mystical and linguistic commentary on Job 28, emphasizing that Divine Wisdom is more precious than the finest gold or gemstones and remains beyond human grasp.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the philosophical and mystical exploration of the Divine attributes, specifically focusing on the "Unique" soul and the inaccessible nature of Divine Wisdom (Chokhmah). It transitions from the structural analysis of God's names to a debate between the "Natural Philosophers" and traditional theology regarding the eternity of the universe versus its creation in time.</meta>

-> RAAVAD of Blessed Memory | Chapter 1 | RAMBAM of Blessed Memory <-

### [Right Page]

That which is called the **Unique One** <note>Yechidah: The highest of the five levels of the soul, representing the point of absolute unity with God.</note>, you have known its value according to its aspect; no intellect can grasp it at all, nor in any way. Nor did He send any herald to investigate its existence. As for **Wisdom** <note>Chokhmah: The second Sefirah, often associated with the first flash of divine inspiration or the blueprint of creation.</note>, how it was prepared in the heavens—that which is designated to be reached—one cannot reach the truth of its existence. For when it was attributed to being reached, it was like the secret of the soul—the spirit, the breath, the unique, and the living <note>original: "נפש רוח נפש יחידה וחיה"; refers to the five levels of the soul: Nefesh, Ruach, Neshamah, Chayah, and Yechidah.</note>. Therefore, the land of "reaching" is called the **Land of the Living**.

And even the "Heavens of Heavens" are there, and though one seeks Wisdom from the Land of the Living, he will not reach the truth of its existence. **"The Deep said: 'It is not in me'"** <note>Job 28:14</note>. This means: if one comes to investigate the essence of Wisdom—how its value is reached from within the "Deep" of reaching—we cannot succeed. For the Deep testifies about itself that there is no providence in it at all, for there is no attainment of its value. If so, how can the one attempting to grasp it succeed from within the Deep?

And when the Deep is compared to the "Three Heads" <note>likely referring to the three "Mother Letters" or the components of the Divine Chariot</note>, almost like the Beginning <note>Bereishit</note>, meaning: the **Face of a Man**, the **Face of a Lion**, and the **Face of an Eagle**. And behold, in my writings, I have chosen the secret of the Deep and the levels of conquest to investigate what is within your capacity to know. Regarding how the value of Wisdom is in its "reaching," we still cannot attain its existence. For behold, there is Wisdom which is a **Sea without end**, and the "Dust" testifies about itself that it has no value compared to it. If so, how can we reach the ultimate purpose of its source?

**"Fine gold cannot be given in its place"** <note>Job 28:15</note>. The meaning is that its "Lock" <note>A play on the word "Segor" (fine gold/lock)</note> is my doubt. **"Pure"** means: if it is in his heart to take its value to reach it, and from all the "Strength" <note>Gevurah</note> of reaching; and because the intellect of reaching is called "Locked" (meaning hidden). **"And silver shall not be weighed as its price"**—this is a parable for everything. **"It cannot be valued in the gold of Ophir"** <note>Job 28:16</note>. And heat in the source of the value of Wisdom from within the **Tiferet** <note>Beauty/Harmony: The central Sefirah</note>, which preceded the gold of Ophir, is comparable to every source of Judgment that is destined to be drawn out... precious and sapphire, and within the Tiferet are two daughters to their essence, and to the Ten Paths.

<meta>catchword: bound together</meta>

---

### [Left Page]

The **Natural Philosophers** <note>Chachmei Ha-Teva: Medieval term for scientists/Aristotelian philosophers</note> argue for the eternity of the world <note>original: "מקדמותו"; the belief that the universe has always existed and was not created at a specific point in time.</note>, as the **Guide** <note>Maimonides, in his "Guide for the Perplexed"</note> wrote in Part Two, Chapter 16: "And these are his words: 'If this question had been clarified by a conclusive demonstration, there would be no need for one who preceded in nature to believe in it, nor would there be a need to fight everyone who says something that is far-fetched. For everything that has been proven by demonstration cannot be added to, nor can the truth be filled by the disagreement of those who oppose it.'" 

We hear from this that if he had a way to destroy the [argument] of eternity, he would have done so by way of his qualities, and not [rely solely on] the Ancient One. He dwells among us with two Names, for with **YaH** <note>The two-letter name of God, Yod and Heh</note> the Lord formed the worlds <note>Isaiah 26:4</note>. And there was no end and end, and not everything. With six letters, the Holy One created Heaven and Earth, and similarly, six worlds surround the world. The world was created with **Kindness** <note>Chesed</note>, and this world was, and this is the meaning of "In the beginning" <note>Bereshit</note>—in the creation of the dwelling, as it is said regarding two worlds.

And our Sages of blessed memory said in **Genesis Rabbah**: "Why did the Torah begin with the letter *Bet*? Because it represents two worlds <note>The Hebrew letter 'ב' (Bet) has the numerical value of two.</note>, created with the powers of the God of Israel," etc. This is because He wants to count the help of His story through the **Ten Names** of the Holy One. And he took the name **YHVH** according to the order of the verse. And this is first because we "ascend in holiness" and do not descend. For if he had placed *YHVH* first and afterward *YaH*, it would have been the opposite of the intellectual order. 

And **Israel** was not created except that the thought of the Holy One preceded everything regarding Israel. As the **Gaon Rabbeinu Saadia** <note>Saadia Gaon (882–942), a foundational figure in Jewish philosophy.</note> explained: "Behold, this speaks with me." Thus explained the Gaon. **Rabbi Moshe Botril** <note>A 14th-century commentator on Sefer Yetzirah.</note> said... supported by **Zion the Prince, Rabbi Shmuel** <note>Likely referring to Samuel Ha-Nagid.</note>...

<meta>This page continues the commentary on Maimonides' Guide for the Perplexed, Part 1, Chapter 57. The previous page explored the concept of the "Unique soul" and the inaccessible nature of Divine Wisdom. This page transitions into a specific discussion of Divine attributes, specifically the attribute of "Life" (Chay) and how human language fails to capture the Divine essence.</meta>

# Shem Tov &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Chapter 57 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; The Efodi

<note>This page features a layout common in Rabbinic bibles and philosophical texts: Maimonides’ primary text is interpreted through two distinct lenses. On the right is Shem Tov, who focuses on the logic and linguistics of the attributes; on the left is the Efodi, who often incorporates Aramaic translations and poetic imagery to explain the difficulty of attaining wisdom.</note>

### [Right Column: Shem Tov]

The explanation in the **Arabic language** <note>original: "בלשון ערבי"; Maimonides originally wrote the *Guide* in Judeo-Arabic.</note> for the term <term>Khaliq</term> <note>meaning "Creator" or "Determiner"</note> suggests something established and powerful. Regarding this doubling, it is like the term for "order" or "arrangement" used in military contexts. The Master <note>Maimonides</note> will explain this further in the upcoming chapter.

There is a manuscript version that reads **"Living"** before the word **"Existent."** This is a better reading. Therefore, the Master brings proof for the lack of composition <note>the idea that God is a simple unity, not made of parts</note> in God. Throughout his commentary, he uses the phrase **"The Living God"** <note>original: "אלקים חיים" (Elohim Chayim)</note> to show that His life is His essence. 

There is another version that says "The Living One, the Sufficient." This is a subject of deep study in the commentary of Rabbi Samuel <note>likely Samuel ibn Tibbon, the primary translator of the *Guide* into Hebrew</note>, who explained that "Sufficient" <term>Shaddai</term> relates to the idea that He is the source of all life. 

However, I say that according to the literal path of logic, the first version is more correct. We do not find anywhere else the term "The Living" used as a name on its own, but rather "The Living God." This is because the **Divine Essence** <note>original: "העצם האלקי"</note> does not receive attributes in the way a physical body does. A physical body only "receives" these descriptions as external accidents, but the Divine Essence is exalted and uplifted above them. 

When we say He is "Living," our intention is not the same as when we describe ourselves as "living." We have never known "life" except through our own human experience of **sensation and motion**. If that is the case, when the text says "The Living God," it is contrasting Him against the gods of the nations, who are described as "dead" <note>idols of wood and stone</note>. This is meant to negate from Him the opposite of life (death), not to define His life in the way our minds understand it. For our minds cannot grasp life except as something involving feeling and movement. 

It has already been clarified in **Pure Philosophy** <note>referring to Aristotelian metaphysics</note> that the Blessed One is transcendent above such things. Therefore, the Sages say He is "Living" to negate from Him the attribute of death. In this way, we also say He is **One** to negate plurality, not to define His "Oneness" according to the human concept of a single unit. For the Blessed One is not a "soul," nor is He defined by parts or by attachment.

<column-break/>

### [Left Column: Efodi]

...with the intellect and according to the perception of the imagination in this place. One cannot "compare" to it <note>referring to Wisdom</note>. It is like a flow of pure glass; everything comes from this source. Even vessels of fine gold from every foundation are as nothing compared to it. It is said: **"Man knows not its value"** <note>Job 28:13</note>. This means no one knows the true rank and arrangement of how Wisdom exists.

**"Coral and crystal"** <note>original: "ראמות וגביש"; Job 28:18</note>. These heights of wisdom have no comparison to the source. It is forbidden to investigate their inner nature too deeply, as will be explained later. Therefore, "silence is praise to You." 

However, the **"Acquisition of Wisdom is above pearls"** <note>Job 28:18</note>. This refers to the drawing down of the attributes of the spheres. Just as the essence of Life is not perceived by any other, so too Wisdom is not perceived by any other. **"Ramot"** (Coral) may also be derived from the word for "Exalted" <term>Rom</term>. This means the loftiness of the **Cause of Causes** <note>God</note> is not something to be sought or requested through human logic. 

Indeed, the verse says: **"The Deep says: 'It is not in me'"** <note>Job 28:14</note>. The heights of the Exalted One—how can they be measured? It is impossible. **"It cannot be valued with the gold of Ophir"** <note>Job 28:16</note>. For even the language of the **Aramaic Translation** <note>The Targum</note> says that if you come to investigate the value of Wisdom in the Cause of Causes, you will find it is spread out beyond your reach. 

Wisdom is more precious than anything else. Regarding the **"Gold of Ophir"** mentioned here, it is from the secret of the "Beautiful" <note>referring to a specific mystical or geographic quality</note>. Even if one had the purest, cleanest, and most refined gold, it still could not reach the value of Wisdom. 

Therefore, the translation of the verse **"It cannot be exchanged for pure gold"** <note>Job 28:15</note> in the Aramaic says: "It shall not be given in exchange for fine gold, and silver shall not be weighed as its price." <note>original: "לא יתיהב דהבא סנינא חלופיה ולא מיתקל כספא דמינה"</note>. You cannot trade for it with the most precious gold of Ophir or with the most expensive jewels. The translation concludes: "A wealthy man cannot arrange its value with his riches; its price is beyond all comparison."

<summary>This page explores the philosophical problem of attributing "Life" to God, arguing that it is a negative attribute used to deny death rather than a description of biological function. It also uses the poetic imagery of Job 28 to emphasize that Divine Wisdom is inaccessible and more valuable than any physical treasure.</summary>

<keywords>Attributes of God, Negative Theology, Divine Life, Wisdom, Job 28, Shem Tov, Efodi, Arabic Translation, Gold of Ophir, Cause of Causes</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Sefer Yetzirah (Book of Creation), specifically focusing on the first chapter. The previous discussion likely touched upon the initial emanations of the ten Sephirot (Divine attributes). This page explores the unreachable nature of Supreme Wisdom (Chokhmah) and moves into a philosophical defense of the creation of the world, citing both Kabbalists like the Ra'avad and Ramban, and philosophers like Maimonides and Aristotle.</meta>

# The Ra'avad, of blessed memory &nbsp;&nbsp; Chapter 1 &nbsp;&nbsp; The Ramban, of blessed memory
<note>The Ra'avad (Rabbi Abraham ben David) and Ramban (Nachmanides) are two of the most significant medieval commentators; here their views are synthesized in a commentary on Sefer Yetzirah.</note>

...called "Force." Know that ultimately, one can assign it a value only insofar as it relates to the end of a general category, but not to the end of a specific detail. Furthermore, one cannot truly investigate the end of the Spirit <note>the Divine Breath/Ruach</note> to research the existence of its Wisdom—how it existed within the particular Waters <note>a reference to the primordial elements of creation</note> belonging to it. One cannot reach the "encampments" of its existence, for the **Supreme Crown** <note>original: "כ"ע" (Keter Elyon), the highest Sephirah</note> is associated with the secret of the five levels of the soul: **Nefesh** [Vital Soul], **Ruach** [Spirit], **Neshamah** [Breath], **Chaya** [Living Soul], and **Yechidah** [Unique Soul].

Therefore, the "Land" of the Supreme Crown is called the **Land of Life**. Even an achiever who is within it, who desires to seek out Wisdom from the Land of Life, will not reach the encampments of its existence. Regarding this, the verse says: **"The Abyss said, 'It is not in me'"** <note>Job 28:14</note>. This means that if one comes to investigate the essence of Wisdom—how it is valued within the Supreme Crown from the perspective of the "Abyss" of the Supreme Crown—he will not be able to perceive it. For the Abyss testifies of itself that Wisdom is not within it. The reason is that there is no perception of its value within the Supreme Crown. If so, how can the perceiver grasp it from within the Abyss? 

The Abyss is likened to the "three heads of the leopard" <note>an esoteric reference to the Chariot vision or the "three heads" mentioned in the Zohar</note> when enveloped in Mercy—meaning the face of a human, the face of a lion, and the face of an eagle. This is found in the writings: that without the secret of the Abyss and its essence, and the "Name of Glory," one cannot investigate what is drawn down from the Supreme Crown to know the value of Wisdom. We still cannot perceive its existence because the Sea of Wisdom is a sea without end. Nevertheless, it testifies of itself that its value is not with it. If so, how can one reach the ultimate goal of its investigation?

**"Fine gold shall not be given in its place"** <note>Job 28:15</note>—this refers to gold that is clean and pure. The explanation is: if we come to take its value in the Supreme Crown from the side of **Gevurah** [Strength/Judgment] within it (for power from that side is called "Closed," meaning a "treasure" <note>a play on the word *Sagur* (closed gold/treasure)</note>), **"nor shall silver be weighed as its price."** This refers to the power from the Supreme Crown—meaning it is "uplifted" <note>original: "נס" (Nes)</note> from all lack. **"It cannot be valued with the gold of Ophir"** <note>Job 28:16; original: "כתם אופיר" (Ketem Ophir)</note>. 

And if we investigate the value of Wisdom from within the **Tiferet** [Beauty/Harmony], which is likened to the gold of Ophir, it is similar to the source of Judgment that is destined to be drawn into it from the side of the "precious garment and sapphire." In the verse, it is written that this refers to its very essence and to the **Atarah** [Crown/Malchut] which is clung to...

<column-break/>

...as a created being.

The natural philosophers who preceded him—such as the author of **The Guide** <note>Maimonides</note> in Part 2, Chapters 15 and 7—noted: had this matter been clarified by absolute proofs, there would have been no need to rely on the opinions of the naturalists who preceded him. Nor would he have needed to say what he said in a place where he differs from them. My own opinion is that anything proven by demonstration cannot be added to, and the Truth does not fall because the scholars of the world disagree with it. 

Hear what they asked of **Aristotle** in Babylon regarding the "novelty" <note>creation ex nihilo</note> of the world. He was [consistent] in the manner of his causal arguments and did not precede them. Regarding this, the Lord blessed us with two Names <note>YHVH and Yah</note>. He created the world as it is said: **"For in Yah [Y-H] the Lord [YHVH] is the Rock of Worlds"** <note>Isaiah 26:4; original: "כי ביה יי' צור עולמים"</note>. The Lord [YHVH] was the end, and he added... with six letters He created, which preceded Heaven and Earth. 

"Worlds" [Olamim] refers to the two worlds: this world, which was created with the letter **He**, and the World to Come, which was created with the letter **Yod**. We find that the word **Bereshit** ["In the beginning"] combined with these intentions refers to these two worlds. Our Sages of blessed memory asked: Why did the Torah begin with the letter **Bet**? It is on account of the two worlds. 

Regarding "the powers of the God of Israel," etc.—this is because he wants to count His story in the book as ten holy names. He follows the order of the verse. Furthermore, placing them at the beginning is because "we rise in holiness and do not descend." If he had placed the name **YHVH** first and **Yah** afterward, the thought would have been reversed. They said: Had Israel not been created, [the world would not exist]. The assembly of holiness preceded everything for the sake of Israel. Our Sages said in the Midrash: "The word *Reshit* [Beginning] refers to nothing other than Israel." 

That which was said, "Forever, let My word speak with me"—so explained the Gaon, **Rabbi Saadia**. 

**Moses Botarel** said: I was the son of Sikhon the Prince, **Rabbi Samuel** <note>likely Samuel ibn Tibbon</note> who explained...

# The Ramban, of blessed memory &nbsp;&nbsp; Chapter 1 &nbsp;&nbsp; The Ra'avad, of blessed memory
<note>original header: "הרמב זל פרק א הראבד זל". The Ra'avad (Rabbi Abraham ben David) and Ramban (Nachmanides) are two pillars of medieval Jewish thought. This manuscript presents their commentaries alongside one another.</note>

### [Commentary of the Ra'avad, of blessed memory]

And as it is explained, the meaning of **Kindness** <note>Chesed, the fourth Sephirah</note> is established by measure. As it is said: *“Who has measured the waters in the hollow of his hand...”* <note>Isaiah 40:12</note>. When He makes a decree for the rain, determining how much rain and early rain shall fall, and how much shall be disqualified or destructive in judgment according to the variation of the rain. And it is the way to envision the Constellation of Might <note>Mazzal HaGevurah</note>; voices whisper to Him and He "recounts" them—meaning He arranges the place of **Understanding** <note>Binah</note> for **Wisdom** <note>Chokhmah</note>. And it also occurs in action. *“And He said to man”* <insert>the prayer</insert> *“Behold, the fear of the Lord”* <insert>the crown</insert> *“is wisdom, and to depart from evil is understanding”* <note>Job 28:28</note>. 

And the Holy One, Blessed be He, granted us the power to perceive them only through the fear of the Lord, which consists of **Ten Sephirot** <note>Divine emanations</note> through the four <note>letters of the Name</note>, as we learned regarding the Sephirot <margin>paths</margin>: *“their beginning is in their end and their end is in their beginning.”* And in the foundation of the secret lies the reason for **Understanding**; there is no power to perceive it except through that which is above all permutations. As it is written: *“to depart from evil is understanding.”* 

This is the "golem" <note>the raw material or unformed substance</note> of all that is explained, for the **Understanding** was the finish of the writing within them, by engraving them within her, and their vitality is in the forms of all specific details and generalities. These are the very shapes of the **Letters**, one inside the other. **Aleph** <note>the first letter</note> said "Aleph" until it reached a "thousand thousands" <note>original: "אלוף אלפים"; a wordplay on the letter Aleph and the word 'Eleph' (thousand)</note>. They follow their custom and are engraved one inside the other according to the space of their expansion and all the other letters. 

Such is the form of all the generalities of the "inheritances" and their details, and the details of their details. Such is the form of every single blade of grass. This means the specific intention for the details of the grasses, numbering **one hundred and sixty-one thousand** <note>a traditional Kabbalistic number for the species of flora</note>. And the word "Aleph" <note>1,000</note> is "Aph" <note>90</note> plus a simple "Lamed" and "Yod" <note>numerology/Gematria calculation</note>, totaling nine hundred, and the space between the two makes two thousand one hundred. 

Such are the details of the inanimate, and the details of the flora for all trees and distances. Likewise, the details of the beasts, birds, domesticated animals, elders, creeping things, and the fish of the sea. Likewise, all the vitality of the species of **Angels** and their ten classes and their dwellings. Likewise, the spheres, the stars, and their constellations, and their minute details. Likewise, the hidden and composite species from the wild and from the elements, according to the variation—all of them are written. They were all included in **Three Books** <note>Sepharim</note>, which are the Three Names <note>referring to the opening of Sefer Yetzirah: "with three books: with text (Sepher), with number (Sephar), and with communication (Sippur)"</note>.

---

### [Commentary of the Ramban, of blessed memory]

From within it, we rise to a place for all the heights above. **He who dwells forever** <note>Shochen Ad</note>: the secret of our speech, for He has a head but no end. **And Holy is His Name**: for His Name is Himself and Himself is His Name, and everything is Holy. Moses said in the morning <note>during prayer</note> that He raised him up by the **Knot of the Crown** <note>the knot of the Tefillin/phylacteries</note> so that He might come to speak to the "hole" <note>the cleft of the rock</note> like a knot. 

And I say for a blessing to our Understanding: He mentioned the end of the **Ten Names** of the Holy One, Blessed be He, corresponding to the ten thoughts that are in the world in general. And there is a great secret in the calculation of the **Tenth**, as mentioned by the Master of Kabbalah in the **Book of Ezra** <note>likely referring to the circle of Rabbi Ezra of Gerona</note>. And behold, in the "Sacred Services" he explained the secret of this chain: in the calculation of the tenth, there is a movement for reincarnation <note>Gilgul</note>, and "the tenth shall be holy." 

Movement in the **Knot of Peace**, like the mercy of a dream, "and the tenth shall be holy." Movement of the powers of the scribe. The Merciful One who awakens between the "sweetness of the juices." And its danger is the tithe <note>Asur/ten</note> and the Holy Voice. These are the **Ten Powers** revealed from His power, like Kindness, as a word in the book regarding the matter of the "watcher" in the essential vitality, which are witnesses signed upon his birth. 

And to hide this matter, when it fell to the "Wood-cutter" of the Holy City: on the day of **Shemini Atzeret** <note>the eighth day of assembly</note>, it shall be removed for you and not a crown <note>Ateret</note>. And in his merit, the dream clings to the edge, and the kingdom was in the royal garment of Kindness for you, the King, and a royal crown was placed on his head. For "Crown" (*Ateret*) is a language of royalty... This is the wondrous secret that the Sages of blessed memory established to say the verse: *“And my heart blessed the King in the house of the palace...”* 

When a wise man begins in the **Chosen House** <note>The Holy Temple</note> to awaken the Seraph and the flow from the mysteries... a dividing screen to perceive the Truth. And all of you who gaze all your days, the place of his study is in the **Torah**, which is written in the **Ten Commandments**. And when this permutation passes in its calculation, there is a great secret in the **Living Soul** <note>Nefesh Chayah</note> regarding every thing that is holy to Him, and it is in less than ten. And to hint at this great ox...

# The Ra'avad, of blessed memory &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Chapter 1 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; The Rambam, of blessed memory
<note>original header: "הדאבר ולו פרק א הרמב זל". "The Ra'avad" (Rabbi Abraham ben David) and "The Rambam" (Maimonides) are cited here as the primary authorities, though the text blends their philosophical and mystical approaches.</note>

<margin>
Chapter 1
</margin>

It is not possible to gaze upon the foolishness [of those who try to quantify it], but rather through the glory as He sustained it, so that we should not be troubled or misled. How is the explanation of the Hebrew alphabet <note>original: "אבג'"</note> to be understood concerning the Kings of the Corners? Love and the Prophet are not mentioned, nor did the different kinds of colorful garments and precious silks <note>original: "פפונים וערכלין ובירוזין"</note> stand. He will not mention them, and in contrast, they are lower than pearls. **It cannot be valued** <note>original: "לא יערכנה"</note> with the topaz of Ethiopia, nor can it be weighed against pure gold <note>Job 28:19</note>. It shall not be compared to pearls or the red stones that come from there. In its pure valuation, you shall not be pampered; it is kept away from you by the withholding of Wisdom. And its value is not like a helpmate.

And it is explained to you that even after one who merits to investigate its foundation on his own—outside the reception of the Tradition <note>Kabbalah</note> and hearing the Word—he says that even this is withheld from being grasped and scrutinized. Common sages <note>original: "וחכמי' חולין"</note> explain strength, and this is the place to explain that the **Spirit of God** <note>original: "רוח אלדיס"; a common scribal variant to avoid writing the Name 'Elohim'</note> in His wisdom and in His world is hidden from the eyes of all living. This means even from the Sephirot <note>Divine emanations</note> that are below them, and from the birds of the sky, or all the pure Chariots <note>Celestial hierarchies</note>. For there is no male or female in the heavens for them all.

Death and destruction—they are the heads of the "shattering" <note>The 'breaking of the vessels' in Kabbalah</note>, for the walking is the exchange of life. For death is the gift of the connection, and the deficiency spoils the lights; death is the exchange of life. They said in our examinations—meaning by their intention we heard—the hearing of Wisdom belongs to the God <note>original: "אלדיס"</note> of Purity. The intention written in the child <note>unclear: possibly referring to 'Yelud' or 'offspring' of wisdom</note> and called by the name of God who understands its way. This means the Verse is open as a path to the world for each and every one from the strata <note>original: "סטרטיס"; from the Greek 'strata', meaning paved roads or levels</note>. And He knows its place, the place where its paths go. For He looks to the ends of the earth and sees under all the heavens <note>Job 28:24</note>. And because He looks at the earth and under all the heavens, behold, the work of all creatures is detailed in the secret of the birth of the wise. Nothing is hidden from Him. And this is the secret of Wisdom: to make a weight for the spirit of the rectifiers <note>original: "מתקנים"; those who perform Tikkun/rectification</note>, measured by the scale. 

---

It is connected, and therefore he said that all the words of the **Philosophers** <note>original: "הפילוסופים"</note> concerning imaginary things are more about righteousness than they are about truths. He said: "He turned his staff toward him and placed his soul in the Garden of Eden, the garden of life, far from every lack which the honored ones increased." And see the mercies of our examiners who walked to the Sages of Peace, for he would be for you in Wisdom. And I have taken from my strike and from the chain that his soul should not be missing in the manner of our square and our triangle <note>Geometric metaphors for philosophical logic</note>. For many honored sayings regarding our logic—which is the "place" and the "dwelling" of the intellect—the chosen one decreed judgments and righteous words. And the Sages of Israel have already said: "A wise man is better than a prophet" <note>Talmud, Bava Batra 12a</note>. 

And this "Highness" <note>original: "קלוס"; possibly 'kalo' or 'kalos'</note> was the special virtue of the philosophers, for every taste shows the house, even if it is Greek. Perhaps he strove to establish the manner of the crowns with the secret of our examiners. And the **Wisdom of Pure Philosophy** <note>original: "וחכמת הפילוסופיה הטהורס"</note> is bound to the **Wisdom of the Kabbalah** <note>original: "בחכמ' הקבלס"</note>, for the Kabbalists called it "Philosophy." However, the language of Philosophy and the language of Kabbalah are distinct, yet every wise person should be of one will in both the hidden and the plain meaning. 

He hinted at "High and Exalted," as the Prophet said: "Thus says the Lord, High and Exalted" <note>Isaiah 57:15</note>. This refers to a great degree and loftiness, for the Exalted Essence is removed from every accident <note>term: accident; a philosophical term for a non-essential property that can change</note> that clings to matter. Therefore, one should not attribute to Him any change or affectation, for He is not a physical substance. For every physical substance is a carrier for "accidents" and is a recipient of opposites. For truly, destruction comes from the deficiency of the accident which is carried in the substance. But the substance which is non-physical is removed from the definition of any accident. Therefore, there is no lack for it, for it has nothing besides the substance, and the substance has no lack. Therefore, it is not possible for there to be a "heart" there to divide "High" and "Low" as if they were physical places. And thus wrote **The Guide** <note>original: "המורס"; referring to Maimonides' 'Guide for the Perplexed'</note> in Chapter Twenty <note>Part 1, Chapter 20 of the Guide</note> of blessed memory. And to the wise child this is a wonder, for "High" and "Low" do not mean what is commonly understood...

It references the Book of Job and Maimonides to argue that God is an incorporeal substance beyond physical dimensions like "high" or "low."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the first chapter of Sefer Yetzirah (The Book of Formation). While the previous page focused on the biblical text of Job and the hidden nature of Wisdom, this page shifts to a technical analysis of the "Three Sefarim" (three books/tools) through which the world was created, citing major medieval authorities including Maimonides (Rambam), the Ra'avad, and Isaac the Blind.</meta>

# The Rambam, of blessed memory &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Chapter 1 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; The Ra'avad, of blessed memory

<margin>
The Rambam, of blessed memory
</margin>
...and so on. The first book is the secret of the constellation <note>original: "מזל"</note>... In the second book is the secret as mentioned above of "atonement," and the third book is the secret of the "line and thread" <note>original: "קו וחוט"</note>... and the Sages of blessed memory explained here that "Book, Scribe, and Story" <term>Safar, Sefer, and Sippur: These three Hebrew words share the same root (S-P-R) and are used in the opening of Sefer Yetzirah to describe the tools of creation: Text, Number, and Communication.</term> correspond to the secret of the **Three Mothers** <note>The letters Aleph, Mem, and Shin, which represent the primary elements in this system.</note>, the secret of the **Seven Doubles**, and the secret of the **Twelve Elementals**.

And the Master, **Rabbi Isaac the Blind** <note>original: "רבי יצחק סגי נהור" (1160–1235), a foundational figure in Provence Kabbalah.</note>, explained that "Scribe, Story, and Book" correspond to the three [divisions]. His intention was that the "Book" is the matter, and the "Story" refers to the bodies of the created beings. For the "Story"—until the formed body is prepared and fit for that which it was created—cannot exist except through **number, measure, and weight**, and the tradition and the value of movements, and the **arrangement of music**; it is all through number. 

The word "Book" <note>original: "ספר"</note> in the language of "engraving" <note>original: "חרתה"</note> refers to the Intention <note>original: "הכוונה"</note>, meaning that no "house" is rested from His thought until its form has first been modeled in His soul. And He hinted by His name that He remembers and weighs everything. He is "Zakhur the Levite," the voice of the words of the Living God. And remember within it the rests of the movement and the image of that which was mentioned as "power," just as He said, "**Let there be light, and there was light**."

---

<margin>
The Ra'avad, of blessed memory
</margin>
The heart and all the rests. And afterward he said, "In three books," and generally here are the laws of writing... and then he said "By Book, Scribe, and Story," which includes everything mentioned above. 

So too, the name *SS* <note>original: "סס"</note> contains letters, there are four letters, and they are ten alone... and the letter *Yod* by His will is ten in **Wisdom** <note>Chokhmah</note>, and another *He* is for **Understanding** <note>Binah</note>, and the last is for **Prayer**. They have "ends" <note>extremities/directions</note>, and the final *He* is for the **Offering** <note>Terumah</note>, which includes all existing things in the way they were created, and not in the way of "engraving." And finally, you have the secrets of the chapters in their levels:
1. **Paths**
2. **Names**
3. **Letters**
4. **Books** 

That which is on the first level is called "Paths" <note>original: "נתיבות"</note>; on the second level, it is called "Names"; on the third level, "Letters"; and on the fourth level, "**Sefirot**." All of these serve to explain the secret of the 32 famous letters <note>The 32 "Paths of Wisdom" comprising the 10 Sefirot and 22 Hebrew letters.</note>.

---

...the beauty of the light of the firmament. And the word did not rest until the action was found. "Book" <note>Sefer</note> refers to the writing, and the "**Writing of God**" is His hands, and the "Words of God" are His script. The reason for the [letters] *Chet* and *Resh* is the "speech" <note>original: "דברון"</note>. Behold, the Book, the Story, and the Scribe are one single thing in the reality of God, but in the reality of man, they are divided because of the voice—for man thinks in his mind, speaks with his mouth, and writes with his hand. 

The author of the **Kuzari** <note>Rabbi Judah Halevi's 12th-century philosophical defense of Judaism.</note> said concerning the *Sefer Yetzirah*: "I saw the version of the Master, Rabbi Judah Halevi, of blessed memory: 'Book, Story, and Scribe.'" And it is right to say that "both these and those are the words of the Living God." 

And the Master, **Rabbi Eliezer the Elder**, the Kabbalist, wrote in his clarification on *Sefer Yetzirah* regarding the end of the dominion: "The author of *Sefer Yetzirah* said that the Paths of Wisdom are Book, Scribe, and Story." The intention of "Book" is to make known how the world was created—intelligent and more exalted than other creatures. He said that three matters are needed for it: Book, Scribe, and Story. "Book" <note>Sefer</note> is the writing, for through the powers, the powers of existence descended into it. "Scribe" <note>Safar</note> is the number and the appointment... it is only done through number... from the foundation of the Earth... and so the matter continues through all the "Chariots" <note>The *Merkavot* or divine structures.</note>. And "Story" <note>Sippur</note>, for the sages of Wisdom called the beginning "Story," as they are the measurement of power... He gave a spirit into that composition until it performs an action... but not "speaking," for that is only possible for the One who made everything. This is according to the Master, Rabbi Eliezer the Elder, the Kabbalist, of blessed memory.

The author of the *Kuzari*, the wise philosopher, said... <note>The text here becomes a mix of Hebrew and a Judeo-Arabic/Aramaic dialect referring to the "work of the creation" and "investigation."</note> for our Master and teacher taught us in the way of the exalted and the sublime, and not in the way of the heel <note>lowly</note>. Therefore, do not fail to speak and to compose in his book. And my master, **Rabbi Meir son of Todros of Toledo** <note>Rabbi Meir Abulafia (1170–1244), also known as the Ramah, a major Spanish talmudist and scholar.</note>, wrote concerning the word "Book"...

<meta>catchword: לא (not)</meta>

<summary>This page analyzes the foundational "Three Sefarim" of Sefer Yetzirah—Book, Scribe, and Story—explaining how they represent the unity of divine thought, action, and speech compared to the fragmented nature of human communication. It synthesizes opinions from major figures like Isaac the Blind, Judah Halevi, and Meir Abulafia regarding the 32 Paths of Wisdom and the hierarchy of creation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on Chapter 1 of Sefer Yetzirah. While the previous page (according to the notes) referenced the Rambam (Maimonides), the heading on this page specifically names the Ra'avad (Rabbi Abraham ben David) and the Ramban (Nachmanides), two giants of medieval Jewish thought. # The Ra'avad, of blessed memory &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Chapter 1 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; The Ramban, of blessed memory

<margin>
The Ra'avad, of blessed memory
</margin>

"Included, attached, and joined"—all of these refer to the "Paths of the Holy One, blessed be He," which are contained within the "Book, Scribe, and Story" <note>original: "ספר וספר וספור" (Safar, Sefer, and Sippur). These are the three modes of creation: writing, number/calculation, and speech.</note>. This refers to the numerical value <note>Gematria</note> of the letters of the Blessed Name, which is "Life" <note>original: "חיה" (Chayah)</note>, and the Spirit of God in the intellectual soul. It encompasses **Thought**, the **Knower**, and the **Known** <note>This is a famous philosophical triad used to describe God's unity: God is simultaneously the intellect, the subject who thinks, and the object of the thought.</note>. Wisdom and Understanding are deep; everything is taught by the One who knows them all through His "engraving" and His "story." This is explained as "pardon for its addition" <note>A difficult phrase, possibly referring to the overflowing of divine emanation.</note>... a hint is given that the weak is included within God, for "God is with him" <note>Referencing the idea that even the most "weak" or physical aspects of creation are rooted in the Divine.</note>. This is the secret of the foundation... and therefore the "weak" is affected by their actions, as it is said regarding "shoulders and influences."

And so this:

After we have reached this level, you will now be worthy to understand: "Who can number the clouds by wisdom?" <note>Job 38:37</note>, as we were taught above. Behold, "numbering" <note>original: "יספר" (Yisaper)</note> comes from the language of "Story," "My Book," "Scribe," and "Number." This is to say that the [celestial] desire and all its details are through His writing and His engraving in **Wisdom**. It uses the vessels of the Name, in the sense of "included and joined," for the Paths are included within you. The Spirit of the Living God is poured into known structures, which are the **32 Paths** <term>32 Paths of Wisdom: These consist of the 10 Sefirot (divine attributes) and the 22 letters of the Hebrew alphabet, which serve as the "blueprints" for creation.</term> through which the Torah revolves. This says that their vitality and internal structures rest upon hidden meanings based on a "secret." Furthermore, "Who can number the clouds" can be understood in the sense of **Sapphire** <note>original: "ספיר" (Sapir). In Hebrew, the words for "book," "number," "story," and "sapphire" all share the same root, S-P-R, suggesting that the physical world is a brilliant, structured reflection of divine light.</note>. He opened his eyes and understood the material of "This is the book of the generations of Adam" <note>Genesis 5:1</note>, his nature and his comfort from the "Ancient of Days" <note>A term for God from the Book of Daniel</note>... where "the books were opened" <note>Daniel 7:10</note>. This means that from Wisdom, He numbers. Like clay under a seal, He makes a "Book of Remembrance" before Him for those who fear Him and sanctify His Name. You should understand that He mentions here the **32 Wonders of Wisdom**, which are the totality of all thoughts. They were drawn out from the point called "Wonder" <note>original: "פלא" (Pele). In Kabbalah, this refers to the highest, most hidden point of the divine intellect.</note>, which is a part of Wisdom... After that, it says "He engraved," which refers to the **Unity** within the powers. These are the three levels, and within them is everything.

<margin>
The Ramban, of blessed memory
</margin>

The Holy Torah taught: "In every place where I cause my name to be mentioned I will come to you and bless you" <note>Exodus 20:24</note>. This is a hint toward the "ten" <note>A Minyan, the quorum of ten required for communal prayer.</note>. When the Holy One, blessed be He, comes to a synagogue and does not find ten there, He is immediately angry, as it is written: "Why, when I came, was there no man?" <note>Isaiah 50:2</note>. It is not appropriate to say a **Sanctification** <note>original: "קדושה" (Kedushah), a central prayer</note> with fewer than ten. This is the secret of why the world stands and is sustained. Therefore, "God searched the place." Because of the secret of the "great inclusion"... you should prepare your supplication... this secret is very great, for it indicates the cause of the **Unity of the Greatness of the Holy One, blessed be He**, who is the **"Place of the world, but the world is not His place"** <note>A classic Rabbinic paradox: God contains the universe, but the universe cannot contain Him.</note>. Regarding this it is said: "Behold, there is a place by Me" <note>Exodus 33:21</note>. The "Place," which is the world, is secondary to Him... and his numbering is the number mentioned in the combination of the word "Place" <note>original: "מקום" (Makom). The gematria of Makom is 186. The square of the letters of the Tetragrammaton (10², 5², 6², 5²) also equals 186.</note>. Therefore, the Sages of blessed memory called the Holy One, blessed be He, "The Place" <note>original: "המקום" (HaMakom)</note>, and they say: "Blessed is The Place, Blessed is He." Regarding this, it is said that a Sukkah should not be less than **ten handbreadths** high.

Regarding "With three books: Safar, Sefer, and Sippur": "Book" <note>Sefer</note> refers to an actual book, the attribute of God in the attribute of **Crown** <note>Keter, the highest Sefirah</note>, for although there is no "writing" before Him, nevertheless, everything is written in a book, as it is said: "The Judge sat and the books were opened." "Scribe" <note>Safar</note> refers to "numbering" or "calculation," as it is said that God "counts" and "numbers." "Story" <note>Sippur</note> refers to the "Speaker," the masculine principle. This means the Holy One, blessed be He, combined, weighed, created, and changed... through these three "books."

It explains the "Three Books" (Text, Number, and Speech) and connects the presence of a prayer quorum (Minyan) to the divine attribute of "Place" (Makom).</summary>

# Page 29
### Chapter 1: The Ramban <note>Rabbi Moses ben Nachman, a 13th-century commentator and Kabbalist</note>, of blessed memory

And these are **Book, Scribe, and Story**, which are hints to His three intentions. It is written according to the law of the Beginning <note>Genesis</note>: "These are the generations of the heavens and the earth when they were created" <note>Genesis 2:4; original: "אלה תולדות השמים והארץ בהבראם"</note>. Our Sages, of blessed memory, said: "By the letter *Heh* (ה) the world was created" <note>A Midrashic idea based on a wordplay where "be-hibaram" (when they were created) is read as "be-Heh baram" (He created them with the letter Heh)</note>. Within the existence of the letters are three Names, and this is the secret of the Torah as it is said: "In the beginning God created the heavens and the earth." And so it was said regarding the salvation of "The Lord God, earth and heaven" <note>original: "יי' אלהים ארץ ושמים"</note>, and the sustenance of the world: "By the word of the Lord the heavens were made, and by the breath of His mouth all their host."

And this is the secret of what was said: there are three existences <note>original: "הוויות" (havayot)</note>... and by the breath of His mouth which is the hidden and ascending essence. Its letters ascend to the number 600 <note>The numeric value, or Gematria</note>, which is the beginning of the world as explained by our teacher Saadia Gaon <note>A 10th-century philosopher and scholar</note>, may his memory be a blessing. And the 22 letters are divided into... <note>The text here enters a complex technical discussion of Gematria and letter permutations</note> and the combination of their weight is 430, and another 850, and a portion of 460 into three parts, and let each part be from the letter *Aleph* (א). From here, through the combination of the 22 letters, how does one say that the letter *Heh* (ה) is exchanged in combination with *Yod* (י)? ... And the *Kuf* (ק) is exchanged with the combination of *Vav* (ו) ... These are the three existences in the letter *Heh*.

And he hinted at this: the great secret of the Holy Torah—"Hear, O Israel: The Lord our God, the Lord is One" <note>The *Shema* prayer</note>. He is hidden in the seven and the three. "Holy, Holy, Holy" <note>original: "קדוש קדוש קדוש"</note>, and this is one secret. Arrange the Menorah <note>The seven-branched candelabrum</note> which is the secret of Kindness. And therefore, the *Ayin* (ע) of the word *Shema* is written large, and the *Dalet* (ד) of the word *Echad* <note>One</note> is written large, forming the word *Ed* <note>Witness; original: "עד"</note>, for He is the witness in His kind and His truth. And this is because with the *Yod* of His Name He established the heavens.

And the "Truth" <note>Emet</note> of God is "Living God and King of the World." It is written: "The beginning of Your word is truth" <note>Psalm 119:160</note>. The Sages said that the seal of the Holy One, blessed be He, is **Truth** <note>original: "אמת" (Emet)</note>. As our Sages explained in the tractates Sanhedrin and Yoma, He is "living" in His essence, and He is at the beginning, the middle, and the end. For the letter *Aleph* (א) is at the beginning of the alphabet, the *Mem* (מ) is in the middle with the final letters <note>original: "מנצפ"ך" (Mantze-pakh) - the five letters that change shape at the end of a word</note>, and the *Tav* (ת) is at the end. And He, may He be blessed, is the completion of His creatures in that "Truth" which God created to do. To hint at this secret, we write here:

->The Great Eagle <note>A title for Maimonides</note>—may he live—and the Rav <note>Nachmanides</note>, my teacher, of blessed memory,<-
->explained *Book, Scribe, and Story* as the "God-born"<-
->meaning: through these three Names the world was created.<-
->And the Rav Recanati <note>Menahem Recanati, a 13th-14th century Italian Kabbalist</note>, of blessed memory, explained *Book, Scribe,<-
->and Story* as: Concept, Thinker, and the Conceivability.<-
->And Abraham our father was called<-

From every "Book and Story," its six letters correspond to the Ten Sefirot <note>The ten divine emanations/attributes in Kabbalah</note>. Thus far the words of Rabbi Eliezer Skali <note>A 16th-century Kabbalist from Morocco</note>, of blessed memory.

<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on the opening of Sefer Yetzirah, moving from the general theory of the "three books" (Sefer, Sapur, Sippur) to their specific mapping onto the Sefirot (divine emanations).
