# The Maimonides <note>original: הרמב"ם (Rambam); though the header mentions him, the primary text is the commentary of the Ra'avad.</note>, of blessed memory — Chapter 1, Mishnah 3 — The Ra’avad, of blessed memory

Of blessed memory, in his composition: His determination is like His determination <note>original: הכרתהו הוא כהכרתו (hachrato hu ke-hachrato); refers to the divine decision or balance.</note>. From the language of "their fibers" <note>original: נימותיהם (nimmoteihem); likely a reference to anatomical or weaver’s threads.</note>, it is not present in the white, it is not present as a likeness. And "cut them off with the sea of death." Therefore, the "white" is called "merchandise" and "greenish white." So it is with the masters of determination, according to the language of "joined together" as in the chapter on "Uncircumcised" <note>original: ערלות (Arlot); a reference to tractate Yevamot or the laws of forbidden fruit.</note>: The uncircumcision of the heresies <note>original: ערל הכפירות (Aral ha-Kefirot); a metaphorical "barrier" of denial or heresy.</note>, even though every sphere of the spheres of the lips: for the matter of his words is like the gathering of his speech. The heresies are simple; nevertheless, "the sickness of water is upon him," "the heart of forgiveness," and "the mediator of sickness" <note>original: וחלוי מים עליו לב סלוח וסרסר חלוה; these are difficult mystical allusions to the softening of the heart and the flow of divine influence.</note>. Like a transformation of intent, for "thoughts of sin are harder than the sin itself" <note>A famous Talmudic maxim: הרהורי עבירה קשים מעבירה.</note>. Uncircumcision of the nose... lack is told to him... lack of the right hand and compassion. "And you shall treat its fruit as uncircumcised" <note>original: וערלתם ערלתו את פריו; quoting Leviticus 19:23.</note>... a company of the moist, and the rod was changed. Determination and birth. The uncircumcision of the tongue is toward its interior, for love... but the determination is not so. And the Master of Tradition <note>original: בעל הקבלה (Ba'al ha-Kabbalah); a generic term for a recognized mystic.</note> in the book *Hekhal Hashem* <note>The Temple of the Lord.</note> wrote, and these are his words: "The determination is a standing secret... hinting at the three existences: Was, Is, and Will Be." This is the end of his words.

And the Great Kabbalist, the Rabbi, Rabbi <unclear>Karnel</unclear> <note>Likely a corruption of a name like Rabbi Isaac the Blind or similar.</note>, wrote: "The covenant of the Unique One," which is to say, "the thought of Unity for the mouth and the tongue." The covenant of circumcision <note>original: ברית מולה (Brit Milah).</note> corresponds to the toes of the feet; thus the "denials" <note>kefirot</note> and the "Unique One" and the world is balanced in them all. On the Throne of His Glory He performs every work, and this is the "nakedness" as it is written: "In order to look upon their nakedness." And until almost... and the secret of the language of "pouring from one vessel to another," for the determination of his spreading out was recognized toward the female... up to here are his words.

Ten toes on the feet, just as there are ten fingers. Each joint of them is comprised of ten "simple" anatomical parts, for there exists in every single limb, in terms of its composition:
(1) Cartilage, (2) Nerves, (3) Bones, (4) Pulsing veins <note>Arteries</note>, (5) Non-pulsing veins <note>Veins</note>, (6) Flesh, (7) Fat, (8) Marrow, (9) Membranes, and (10) corresponding to the heart.
The thirty-two joints <note>L"B (32) joints corresponding to the 32 Paths of Wisdom.</note>

---

...correspond to the Upper World and the Lower World. And just as through the simple organs all the limbs of the body are bound together to become one body and one flesh, until one leg of them spreads through all the limbs and the knowledge of the Sefirot—so too are the Ten Sefirot unified and integrated through the "simple ones," the "mediators," and the "paths," all becoming one. And this is the secret of what Job said: "And after my skin has been destroyed, yet from my flesh I shall see God" <note>Job 19:26. A foundational verse in Kabbalah suggesting the human body is a map of the divine.</note>. And in the saying: "For in the image of God He made man."

And because common people are occupied with the commandments everywhere, therefore, when they remove or distance themselves, they draw near to the "Building" <note>The Temple</note> and wash their hands and feet to purify them from the heat of the "Mind" <note>original: דעת (Da'at); knowledge or consciousness.</note>, for a "distraction of the mind" <note>original: חילוף הדעת (Chiluf ha-Da'at); a technical term in ritual purity for losing focus or mental presence.</note> invalidates the hands. And regarding this, it is said: "Thus shall the children of Israel eat their bread, defiled among the nations." Understand this. And how much more so when he enters the Tent of Meeting or approaches the Altar. And therefore, water is used upon rising from the bed in the morning, which cancels our [physical] stabilities, for thus the Sages of blessed memory explained: "the spirit of the nullification of the limbs," so that the power of the judgment may be sweetened from him.

-> Sefer Yetzirah 8, 9, 10 <-

<summary>This page explores the mystical connection between the human body's anatomy and the "Covenant of the Tongue" and "Covenant of the Circumcision," arguing that the body is a microcosm of divine wisdom. It utilizes medieval medical concepts—such as the liver as the source of blood and the distinction between arteries and veins—to explain how the Ten Sefirot are physically manifested in the human form.</summary>

It specifically elaborates on the "Ten Sefirot" as they manifest in the ten fingers, ten vowels, and the ten stages of speech.</meta>

# The Ra'avad, of blessed memory | Chapter 1, Mishnah 3

A world is deficient beneath us, for it receives from above and bestows below. Everything follows the order of the **Sefirot** <note>The ten divine emanations or "spheres" in Kabbalah through which the Infinite interacts with the universe.</note> and the **Wheels of the Chariot** <note>original: גלגלי המרכבה (galgalei ha-merkavah); referring to the celestial spheres associated with Ezekiel's vision.</note>, for the ten spheres of the Sphere of **Understanding** <note>original: בינה (Binah)</note> decide between them. In the world of the upper spheres, nothing is lacking—God forbid—of any souls at all. Thus, the side of Understanding is a mediator between the world of **Intellects** <note>original: שכלים (sekhalam); the separate angelic or disembodied intelligences.</note> and the world of the **Throne of Glory**. In the same way, the **tongue** is a mediator—meaning in its speech—between the "deciding world"—meaning thought and **Wisdom** <note>original: חכמה (Chokhmah)</note>—which rouse it to speak in order to bring that which is thought from within to the outside. This is the way of nature: Wisdom arouses the intention for expression.

So too regarding the **Foundation** <note>original: יסוד (Yesod); the Sefirah associated with the procreative organ and the funneling of divine energy.</note>, for it is a mediator between the world of the "Intellectual" and the world of the "Natural." There, they rouse the Foundation to bring the arousal into action, just as the male is born from the power of will and thought; from this, the "special vessel" <note>The reproductive organ.</note> is aroused and filled with the spirit of the partial existence to bring the seed into action, from within to actuality, to create a living being. And so, wonder at how a single power, in itself, is sometimes called "Soul" <note>original: נפש (nefesh)</note> and sometimes called "Intellect" <note>original: שכל (sekhel)</note>, for the words soul and intellect are as described in the pamphlet.

The analogy for this is that the water belonging to the "Divided Stars" are called "Exile" in relation to the **Elements**; and that which is composed of them are called "Complete Rain" in relation to the Intellects. And here, according to the number of the **ten fingers**: five against five. Of them, five are for the **physical senses**: the spirit of the drop <note>Likely referring to the refined physical vitality.</note>... corresponding to them in the **imagination** is a sensory "sealed mouth," for it whispers to the blood... as is appropriate in a dream.

Behold, there are five physical senses and five imaginary sensations. The **Covenant of the Unique One** <note>A reference to God’s unity manifested in the body.</note> is centered in the tongue, for from the power of the brain's inquiry—which judges like the action of the physical and imaginary sensations.

Furthermore, it revolves around the number of the ten fingers, five against five, for these ten movements belong to them:
(1) *Kamatz* (2) *Chirik* (3) *Tzere* (4) *Cholam* (5) *Melop* <note>original: מלופם (melofam); an older term for the Shuruk or U sound.</note> (6) *Patach* (7) *Segol* (8) *Kibbutz* of the lips (9) *Chataf Kamatz* (10) <unclear>Hebah</unclear>. <note>The author is mapping the Hebrew vowel system onto the ten fingers to show how divine structure permeates language.</note> Five against five, and all of them relate to the tongue and the spirit, and are divided by the "cut" <note>The physical articulation or stopping of breath.</note>.

So too are the **five locations of the phonetic origins** <note>The five places in the mouth where letters are formed: throat, palate, tongue, teeth, and lips.</note> of the letters, and they are five: (1) Lip (2) Teeth (3) Tongue (4) Throat (5) <note>The manuscript skips the fifth or merges it.</note> All of them are divided to be unified in the tongue and to be partitioned by the "cut" according to their locations.

And so too at the beginning of "Mentioning from Above":
(1) Reflection (2) Thought (3) Imagination (4) Arousal (5) Desire (6) Spirit (7) Voice (8) Speech (9) Conversation (10) Movement.
It is a "possession" <note>original: קניה (kanyah)</note>, five against five. The Covenant of the Unique One is centered in the word of the tongue for speech, for merit, and as the arouser for action. To the wise man, it hints that they all build from the power of **ten actions**. The power and the Wisdom are like the number of the ten fingers, five against five, from the power of "these are His fingers." As for the rest of them, they are entirely for the "conquering of His hands" and the toes of His feet; they too are fundamentally only ten, for ten includes all commands and all numbers. For an individual to be involved in all of them is to learn to be a "dwelling place" for Him, in which the "skins of his soul" are bound. This is the meaning of "my hidden things are worthy..."

If so, you must understand from there the secret of the five fingers on the right and the five fingers on the left; all of them branch out from the Five, and that is **Beauty** <note>original: תפארת (Tiferet); the central Sefirah that balances Mercy and Judgment.</note>, for it is the beginning of speech. From it, the "males" are divided to every side, to the right hand and the left, and the Covenant of the Unique One is centered in the tongue—meaning the **Bride** <note>original: כלה (Kallah); often referring to the Sefirah of Malkhut (Kingship) or the localized presence of the Divine.</note>—for she is the root of all that branches out.

So, when looking and searching out of awe at their division, you will see that "Many" is not truly "Many," but stems from the Absolute One—meaning **Beauty**. Correspondingly, the beginning of the division returns to being one palm on the right and one palm on the left, and the two palms belong to the two arms, and the two arms are unified in the heart. So too with the feet, the meaning is repeated: the division of the two feet testifies to the **Unity of the Upper World**, and likewise the Unity of the Lower World. And to be—

<sig>N B Y V</sig>

<summary>This page explores the mystical parallelism between the ten Sefirot and the human body, specifically focusing on the "five against five" structure of fingers, toes, and Hebrew vowels. It identifies the tongue and the Sefirah of Beauty (Tiferet) as the central unifying forces that bridge thought, speech, and physical action.</summary>

<meta>Continuity: This page transitions from the general description of the Sefirot as "mediators" to a specific discussion of ritual purity and the physical body as a reflection of the divine ten-fold structure. It connects the practice of hand washing to the "spirit of life" and begins to map the human anatomy—specifically the ten fingers and the biological contributions of parents—onto the ten Sefirot and the elemental letters Aleph, Mem, and Shin.</meta>

# The Ra’avad, of blessed memory | Chapter 1, Mishnah 4

The **Living God** <note>original: אלהים חיים (Elohim Chayim)</note> caused a spirit from the source of life to rest upon him; therefore, he is "moist" <note>Meaning vibrant or spiritually alive.</note> and his merit is established in a place where he reigns like a human being. This is because the **spirit of the dead** <note>original: רוח המתים (ruach ha-metim); a reference to ritual impurity or the "husk" of death.</note> which hovers over people according to their physical nature makes them resemble the dead. Therefore, a person should not receive "waters" <note>Likely referring to spiritual flow or ritual washing.</note> except from one who has already nullified and removed the spirit of the dead from himself.

Thus, it is said that one who **scorns the washing of the hands** <note>original: נטילת ידיים (Netilat Yadayim)</note> will eventually come to poverty, for a poor person is considered as if dead. For this reason, the verse joins the "poor" and the "destitute" <note>original: עני ורש (ani ve-rash)</note>, for man’s oppression begins from his poverty.

Therefore, the **waters before the meal** <term>Mayim Rishonim<gloss>The ritual washing of hands performed before eating bread.</gloss></term> require an "upper vessel," similar to the upper vessel made for atonement—the **Laver** <note>original: כיור (Kiyor); the basin used by priests in the Tabernacle.</note>. However, they do not require a "lower vessel" <note>A bowl to catch the runoff water.</note> because the spirit of impurity has not yet rested upon them. But **soiled hands** <note>Hands after a meal.</note> are a place where the spirit of impurity rests. Consequently, the **final waters** <term>Mayim Acharonim<gloss>The ritual washing of hands after a meal, before the Grace After Meals.</gloss></term> require a "lower vessel" to receive them, so that people do not pass over them. 

Every person of faith needs "waters," for through them there is a reception for the "exchanges" <note>Likely referring to the exchange of spiritual states or energies.</note>. This is why it adds: **"And it came to pass, when Moses held up his hand, that Israel prevailed"** <note>Exodus 17:11</note>. A great wonder arises: why does the Jerusalem Talmud say "his hands" (plural) when the verse says "his hand" (singular)? And does it not also say: **"And his hands were steady [faith] until the going down of the sun"**? <note>Exodus 17:12</note> This is because the "clumsiness of hands" is associated with the night. 

From here the Sages taught that it is forbidden for a man to keep his hands spread toward heaven for three hours. Therefore, one spreads the **right hand** to humble the power of the **left hand**, which is associated with **Isaac** <note>In Kabbalah, Isaac represents the attribute of Gevurah (Strength/Judgment), often associated with the left side.</note>—and I cannot explain further. 

For this reason, the **Priestly Blessing** <note>original: ברכת כהנים (Birkat Kohanim)</note> is performed with the lifting of the hands, corresponding to the **ten fingers**, which correspond to the **Ten Commandments**. So too, Israel trusts in their palms during the "tenth" <note>Referring to the tenth day of the month, Yom Kippur.</note> to sanctify the "seventh" <note>The seventh month, Tishrei.</note>, which is life and strength. This provides forgiveness and atonement for all who turn from transgression. 

The **Day of Atonement** <note>Yom Kippur</note> in the month of Nissan <note>The text here makes a complex symbolic link between the tenth of Tishrei and the tenth of Nissan.</note> is the place of the Passover sacrifice, as it is said: **"On the tenth day of this month they shall take to them every man a lamb, according to the house of their fathers"**. This is a sign for the division and for executing judgments among the portions of the nations. On the Day of Atonement, a deep secret is revealed at the head of all. So too on the Sabbath. The secret of this is that the **Ninth of Av** <note>A day of communal mourning.</note> and the fast were actually fit to be like the Day of Atonement; see the Talmud in Tractate Ta'anit.

These correspond to the number of the **ten fingers**, as it is said: **"When I behold Your heavens, the work of Your fingers, the moon and the stars, which You have established"** <note>Psalm 8:4</note>. If so, I see that the **spheres of the Sefirot** are ten, and they are the work of the "ten fingers." 

And when it says: **"My right hand has spanned the heavens"** <note>Isaiah 48:13</note>, it appears as if they were made with a "span" <note>original: טפח (tefach); though the verse uses "spanned" (tipcha).</note>. Another verse says: **"And the heavens with a span [measured]"** <note>Isaiah 40:12; original: זרת (zeret)</note>. And it also says: **"And the heavens are the work of Your hands"** <note>Psalm 102:26</note>. This suggests they were made with two hands. The truth is that there are **Heavens above Heavens**, each according to its level. 

There are **ten horns** in the ten Sefirot, as it says: **"He had horns coming out of His hand"** <note>Habakkuk 3:4</note>. These hint at the ten kingdoms, the Foundation <note>Yesod</note>, and the "Horns of the Almighty." 

Our Sages, of blessed memory, taught: There are **three partners in man**—his father, his mother, and the Holy One, blessed be He.
1. The **father** provides the "white" seed, from which are formed: 
   (1) the bones, (2) the sinews, (3) the nails, (4) the brain in the head, and (5) the white of the eyes.
2. The **mother** provides the "red" seed, from which are formed: 
   (6) the blood, (7) the flesh, (8) the hair on the head, (9) the skin, and (10) the black [pupil] of the eyes. 

These **three partners** correspond to the **three "Mothers"** <note>The three primary letters of the Hebrew alphabet in Sefer Yetzirah: Aleph (א), Mem (מ), and Shin (ש).</note>: **A-M-S**.
- He called the **Spirit** <note>Air/Aleph</note>: God <note>Binah/Understanding</note>, Life <note>Chokhmah/Wisdom</note>, and Wisdom.
- To the **Water** <note>Mem</note>: one.
- To the **Fire** <note>Shin</note>: one.
And so also with this. See the chapter in the tractate.

The truth regarding the "Ten" is that there are five upper Sefirot. They are in the **Aleph** <note>The letter Aleph representing Air/Spirit.</note>... [referring to] **Tiferet** <note>Beauty</note> and the **Holy Place** <note>original: והק"מ (ve-ha-kodesh mamash?)</note>. For these are actions, like one who opens the depths in the form of rain to receive the upper power. 

The eighteen "Truths" correspond to the Ten: 
(1) **Keter** [Crown], (2) **Chokhmah** [Wisdom], (3) **Chesed** [Kindness], (4) **Tiferet** [Beauty], (5) **Netzach** [Victory]. 
These correspond to the "Truth" in the... Between Wisdom... **Binah** [Understanding] is the decider, which is the **Tongue**. 
And between the two "completions"—this is Tiferet, Netzach, Hod, Yesod, and Malkhut. The **Secret of the Covenant** <note>The Brit/circumcision, associated with Yesod.</note> decides between them. The secret is that it is the recipient from their "brothers." Therefore, the second within it is **Binah**, which is like the tongue in the mouth that brings out what arises in thought. So too, the **Shekhinah** <note>The Divine Presence, associated with Malkhut.</note> decides between the world which is above and between the world...

<meta>catchword: ...world (עולם)</meta>

<summary>This page explores the mystical significance of the number ten, linking the ten fingers of the human hand to the Ten Sefirot and the creation of the heavens. It also details the Rabbinic concept of the "three partners in man," mapping biological components provided by the father and mother onto the primary Hebrew letters Aleph, Mem, and Shin.</summary>

<meta>Continuity: This page continues the anatomical and mystical mapping of the human body onto the divine structure. While the previous page focused on the hands and the ritual of washing, this page moves to the head, specifically the throat and mouth, explaining how the physical organs of speech correspond to the letters of the Divine Name and the creative "Spirit of the Living God."</meta>

# The Ra’avad, of blessed memory | Chapter 1, Mishnah 3

And how are the **upper** and **lower** judged? The upper, the lower, and the **swallowing-house** <term>Pharynx<gloss>The Hebrew "Beit ha-Beluah" refers to the throat or pharynx as the "house of swallowing."</gloss></term>, which is like a **small tongue** <note>original: לשון קטן (lashon katan); the uvula.</note>, as is the tongue itself. All of these are for the purpose of speech and for all the **circulating paths** <note>A reference to the 32 paths of wisdom through which the universe was created, here linked to the breath's movement.</note>. And behold, the **Spirit of the Living God** <note>original: רוח אלהים חיים (Ruach Elohim Chayim)</note>—which moved through the nose, right and left—passes over the **upper waters** that are above the firmament <note>The text notes a missing word here in the original manuscript.</note> which are upon the face of the waters.

This is similar to the **curtain** <note>original: מסך (masach)</note> that separates the upper waters from the lower waters. The spirit descends within us through a hollow in the curve of the swallowing-house, and from there it spreads through all the tendons and all the limbs. The tongue then leads this spirit out through sound, breath, and speech, according to the phonetic groups <note>The Hebrew text lists letters representing different parts of the mouth: Throat (ח), Palate (ג/כ), Tongue (ל), Teeth (ז/ס), and Lips (ב/ו/מ/פ).</note>.

For the **mouth** is in the form of a carved **Yod** <note>The smallest Hebrew letter, often representing a point of origin.</note>, and the **tongue** is in the form of a **Vav** <note>A vertical line representing a conduit or connection.</note>. The interior and the swallowing-house—meaning the uvula hanging at the back of the palate—is in the form of an inverted **Heh**. Thus, it is found that all speech passes through seven "vessels" <note>The various organs of the vocal tract.</note> corresponding to the ten **Sefirot** <term>Sefirot<gloss>The ten divine emanations or attributes in Kabbalah.</gloss></term>, and over the 360 dwellings <note>original: ידור (yidur); likely a reference to the degrees of a circle or astronomical cycles.</note>, and over the thirty-two **circulating paths**.

Therefore, "the remnant of Israel shall not do iniquity, nor speak lies, nor shall a deceitful tongue be found in their mouth" <note>Quoting Zephaniah 3:13.</note>, for the **Lord God is Truth**. This is the secret of the commandment "you shall not lie to one another" <note>Leviticus 19:11.</note>. Just as it is among the ways of the **Permutations** <term>Temurot<gloss>A system of Hebrew letter substitution used in mystical interpretation.</gloss></term>, in this regard:

-> To cling to Him, as it is said of Canaan: In his hand <-
-> are scales of deceit; he loves to oppress, without <-
-> doubt. And this is "And you shall teach <-
-> them to your children," to speak <-
-> of them in words of Torah <-
-> and not in words of <-
-> vanity. <-

<summary>This page provides a mystical anatomy of the human vocal tract, mapping the mouth, tongue, and uvula to the letters of the Divine Name (Y-H-V). It emphasizes that speech is a sacred process fueled by the "Spirit of the Living God," necessitating ethical speech and Torah study.</summary>

<meta>Continuity: This page continues the anatomical and mystical mapping of the human body onto the divine structure. Following the previous page's discussion on the head and the throat, this section delves into the "Covenant of the Tongue" and the "Covenant of the Flesh" (circumcision), exploring how these physical sites serve as the anchors for the Sefirot (divine emanations) and the Hebrew alphabet.</meta>

# The Ra’avad, of blessed memory | Chapter 1, Mishnah 2

Since it is the opening of the soul, it is engraved and all the letters are bound and rooted in the **tongue**. The **Covenant** is cut, just as our Sages of blessed memory said: <note>original: רכח (228), likely a reference to a specific folio or code</note> "He cut a covenant..." <note>A reference to the Sefer Yetzirah 1:3: "He formed a covenant in the midst, in the circumcision of the tongue and the circumcision of the flesh."</note> in the act of **circumcision** <note>original: מילה (Milah), which means both "circumcision" and "word."</note>, in the likeness of the letter **Yod** <note>The smallest Hebrew letter, traditionally associated with the point of creation and the mark of the covenant.</note>. It is depicted that the fetus is formed in the *Kunsas* <term>Matrix/Womb<gloss>The original "kunsas" is a rare term referring to the formative vessel or womb-space.</gloss></term> in the image of all limbs through their spirit, as mentioned before regarding the details of the tongue and the "truth" and the "fire of the right."

The tongue is a likeness, and within it are the categories from below; I am unable to explain the variation of the **Middle Pillar** <note>The central axis of the Sefirotic tree that balances the extremes.</note>. To the male, it is awakened with those around them, and the knife accompanies it during the "observance" of the word of the **tongue** <note>The text is playing on "Milah" meaning both speech and circumcision.</note>, in which everything is bound. Just as with the word of the **genitals** <term>Ma’or<gloss>While "Ma’or" usually means "luminary/light," in Kabbalistic anatomy it refers to the reproductive organ as the "source" or "nakedness" (Me'or).</gloss></term>, in which everything is bound—understand this.

The meaning of the statement, "And the **Covenant of Unity** <note>original: וכרית יחיד (U-khrit yachid)</note> is directed in the middle," refers to the meditation on the **Sefirot** <term>Divine Emanations<gloss>The ten attributes through which the Infinite interacts with the universe.</gloss></term>. They are all arranged beneath them and according to their natures. Therefore, at times, the Attribute of Mercy <note>original: מדת הרחמים (Midat ha-Rachamim)</note> is inverted into Judgment <note>original: דין (Din)</note>, and by means of the "Pivot," the Attribute of Judgment is turned to Mercy.

There is no "absolute simple" state in all the Sefirot, except in the **Ten Sefirot of the Tetragrammaton** <note>original: יקוק (a common substitution for the four-letter Name of God to avoid profanity).</note>. Within **Kindness** <note>original: חסד (Hesed)</note>, there is only the nature of **Water**. Therefore, it is impossible for it to turn into the Attribute of Judgment. Likewise, if the Attribute of **Might** <note>original: גבורה (Gevurah)</note> were unique to **Fire** alone, it would be impossible for it to turn into the Attribute of Mercy, for water is the opposite of fire.

By their nature and agreement, they came to "cut" Kindness. They are at the ultimate distance in existence, each one at the opposite extreme from the other, and they cannot exist in one subject at one moment. Therefore, they hope to be as one. Within [the middle] there is the power of Water and the power of Fire. Therefore, our building and the "Covenant of Unity" descend upon the Sefirot so that the two transformations are unified in one power—that is, in **Beauty** <term>Tiferet<gloss>The central Sefirah that balances the opposing forces of Kindness/Water and Might/Fire.</gloss></term>, which is the middle between **Greatness** (Kindness) and **Might**.

Since it is not according to the "Face," so too the **tongue** decides between the upper completion and the lower worlds. Because they are not according to the "Face," the tongue <insert>the refined</insert> receives and influences. So too, the **Foundation** <note>original: יסוד (Yesod)</note> decides between **Victory** <note>original: נצח (Netzach)</note> and **Splendor** <note>original: הוד (Hod)</note> to make known that they are not according to the "Face." Likewise, **Kingship** <note>original: מלכות (Malchut)</note> receives from all of them to make known the foundation of our Lord, may He be blessed and exalted.

The explanation of "directed" <note>original: מכוונת (mekhuvenet)</note> is "ordered." This is intended from the language of "directing the opening to the sea" in our ascents, meaning "windows" and "channels." The Sages said "directed" <note>feminine</note> and not "directed" <note>masculine</note> to hint at the secret of the **Diadem** <term>Atarah<gloss>The "Atarah" is the crown of the Sefirah of Yesod, often associated with the Shekhinah or the feminine aspect of the divine presence.</gloss></term>. When the Water brings unity between its foundation and the Kindness within it, there is a unity between the Kindness in the Diadem. He says that this unity is "in the middle," meaning **Tiferet**.

And as for "the word of the tongue," it refers to the **speech** included in the tongue, which is the **Shekhinah** <term>Divine Presence<gloss>The feminine, immanent aspect of God that dwells within the world.</gloss></term>. The matter of "the word of the tongue" is to select in his mind sound, spirit, and speech—this is the **Holy Spirit**. It hints at **Wisdom** <note>original: חכמה (Chokhmah)</note>. A "word on my tongue" is like Wisdom and **Understanding** <note>original: בינה (Binah)</note>. This is to say that the "last sound" <note>the final letter of the Name</note> will reside upon Beauty, Understanding, and Wisdom, and the **Supernal Crown** <note>original: כתר עליון (Keter Elyon)</note> in their totality. This is the **Yod-He-Vav** <note>The first three letters of the Divine Name</note> until the entire sequence of levels is unified in the Diadem. This is why he said "directed" <note>feminine form</note> and not "directed" <note>masculine</note>.

When he says "and like the word of the genitals" <note>original: המעור (ha-ma'or)</note>, the commentators explain that this also refers to the speech that is in the hollow of the **Foundation** <note>Yesod</note> toward the Diadem. However, the term *Ma’or* <note>nakedness/genitals</note> is a double reference to speech. Therefore, it is "like the circumcision of the member" from the language of "And Abraham circumcised" <note>Genesis 17:23</note>. The explanation of "in the word which is in the member" is that through **Kindness** <note>Hesed</note> he shall cut from the member—which is the cutting of the **foreskin** <note>original: ערלה (orlah)</note>. These are the "transformations." Then the "Covenant of Unity" is the Diadem within the Foundation, and the "directed" opening is achieved.

"Let there be light" <note>original: יהי מאור (Yehi Ma'or)</note> comes from the language of "cave" <note>me'arah</note> or "attachment." It is connected and bound. Since the "Secret" is bound, the Diadem is bound. Thus the Diadem is bound in the Covenant of the Lord, called "the member" <note>Me'or</note>. For in the unity of the Foundation, it is like the "word of the member." Some explain *Ma'or* from the language of "being stirred up" <note>nit'ar</note> or "awakened." Because the Foundation provides the flow of abundance to the Diadem, it is called "The Awakener." Some explain *Ma'or* as "living." "And a coupling of life in the member" <note>original: ולויית חחי במער; a difficult phrase likely referencing the union of male and female forces</note>. Our Sages said that all of this is within the Kindness that is mixed with the "coupling," meaning the "Living" <note>the active force</note> that cleaves to his wife at the time of the act. Therefore, it says "to his wife" and "they shall become one flesh." Because in the place of the **Altar** <note>the site of circumcision or the divine throne</note> are their "windows" and "paths."

He said "And the Covenant of Unity is directed in the middle" because the lips of the mouth and the upper and lower "produce" <note>referring to speech/fruit of the lips</note>...

<meta>catchword: וק' (And Q...)</meta>

It describes how the central Sefirah, Tiferet, balances the opposing forces of Mercy and Judgment, just as the tongue and the reproductive organ serve as central "pivots" for divine energy in the human body.</summary>

<meta>Continuity: This page continues the commentary on the first chapter of Sefer Yetzirah (The Book of Formation). While the previous page focused on the anatomical symbolism of the tongue and the "Covenant of the Flesh," this page moves to Mishnah 4, which defines the "Ten Sefirot of Nothingness" using the metaphor of the ten fingers and toes, while providing crucial warnings against taking these physical descriptions literally.</meta>

### Commentary of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

"Meaning" (*Ke-lomar*): The phrase "meaning" consists of two words, similar to the word *Alamot* <note>original: עלמות; usually "maidens," but here interpreted as "hidden things" or "worlds."</note> where the "chosen ones" were mentioned on the condition that they were like *Alamot*. Likewise, the word *Kichlamon* <note>original: ככלמון; meaning "disgrace" or "shame."</note> is made of two words: "like all death" (*ke-khol mavet*). <note>The Ra'avad is using medieval linguistic analysis to break down complex words into smaller components to derive their mystical meaning.</note> Similarly, "as if to say" means "nothing more and nothing less." For it says, "**Dwell in wisdom and examine with understanding.**" In this, the great Rabbi Chasdai <note>Likely referring to a predecessor or a specific school of thought known to the author.</note> understood them all and integrated them into the Whole.

Then, the author of *The Book of Formation* begins with the **five fingers of his hands** and explains regarding his **feet** that the "Balance" <note>Referring to the Covenant of the Circumcision mentioned in the previous Mishnah.</note> is the mediator between the ten fingers/toes. All of this is merely an example based on "flesh and blood," for fingers do not actually pertain to the Creator, as it is written: "By the word of the Lord were the heavens made." <note>Psalm 33:6. This is a theological disclaimer: God creates by speech, not with physical hands.</note> The use of "fingers" in all these examples follows the words of the Psalmist: "The work of Your fingers." <note>Psalm 8:3.</note>

Therefore, when he mentions these things, he says, "**Dwell in wisdom,**" meaning: use your own wisdom to realize that everything I have told you about "fingers" and the like is strictly by way of an example. It does not apply to the Creator, may He be blessed, for He is not a body nor a power within a body. "**And examine with understanding,**" meaning: find the truth through your understanding, for that is how it is.

Because our Teacher <note>Referring to the author of the text or a specific authority.</note> was at the head of a generation that was nourished by the "Divine Cause," and some of his contemporaries were somewhat inclined toward the error of thinking of the Divine as a body—albeit a body more exalted than all other bodies—he spoke in this way.

Even though he took "Ten Sefirot," he took them like "Ten Fingers," as it says, "**Dwell in wisdom and examine with understanding.**" Likewise, "Stand a matter upon its clarity" <note>original: בור' (Bore); related to the word for clarity or sorting.</note> and "Restore it to its place." For you have been given permission to grasp this, but he warned **not to cut the plantings.** <term>Cutting the Plantings<gloss>A famous Talmudic metaphor for heresy, specifically the error of separating the Sefirot from their Source or imagining a division within the Godhead.</gloss></term>

"Clarify" (*Bore*) is like "The pit of your testimony." <note>A linguistic comparison to a word for clarity.</note> "Who has clarified the water?" It means to clarify it in your heart so that you do not think of the Holy One, Blessed be He, through the lens of physical imagination. "And stand the matter on its clarity" is like standing a matter on its depth and its root because of the Truth. "**And place the Creator on His foundation,**" meaning: the Holy One, Blessed be He.

<meta>catchword: Has</meta>

***

### Commentary of the Ramban, of blessed memory

Regarding the Foundation, there is no absolute thing. If so, you might say there are nine Sefirot and not ten; therefore, the text says, "**And not nine,**" so as not to separate the Divine Presence from the Sefirot. Nor are there "**ten**" <note>The text here navigates the complex counting of the Sefirot, specifically whether the highest, most hidden crown (Keter) or the lower presence (Shekhinah) should be counted.</note> from the Sefirot in a way that divides them; rather, it is a "Teaching of Understanding." No number stands above them, for all numbers are included in ten—ten and not eleven.

The text comes to warn you not to let it enter your mind to say that just as the "Infinite" (*Ein Sof*) is not graspable even though it is within the category of the Sefirot, so too the "Wondrous Supreme Crown" is not graspable and yet is within the count of the Sefirot. Therefore, it says: "**Ten and not eleven.**" This means the Supreme Crown is [sometimes considered] part of the Sefirot.

It says, "**Dwell in wisdom.**" This means you must look into *Chokhmah* (Wisdom) and its Maker. You need to find the source from the **Prime Matter** of the intellect of the "Infinite." <term>Prime Matter<gloss>Called 'Hyle' in Greek; it refers to the first, formless substance from which all existence was formed.</gloss></term> This is *Binah* (Understanding), which is the "Supreme Joy" engraved by the power of the Prime Matter (*Chokhmah*). How did it come to be? All its lights were acted upon by the power of Wisdom.

Therefore, the Sage said, "**Examine in his Wisdom.**" "**Dwell in Wisdom**" means: wait and draw Understanding forth from Wisdom, for the "**Spirit of God**" is Understanding. "**And examine with Understanding**" means: speak with the power of the "Supreme Joy" within the intellect. One must confess that all the powers within Understanding that are revealed to your eyes through contemplation—all of them came to Understanding from Wisdom.

Therefore, the Sage said, "Dwell in Understanding," and not "Examine in Understanding," for "Dwell in Wisdom" means you must listen to Wisdom, otherwise you cannot act. Therefore, "Turn your ear" means: when you are blessed with Wisdom, you must incline yourself to "Turn your ear" to know from what source within Wisdom the Understanding flows. As it says: "Happy is the man who finds wisdom, and the man who draws out understanding" (Proverbs 3:13). This means he brings Understanding out from within Wisdom. And it says: "The beginning of wisdom is: get wisdom; and with all your getting, get understanding" (Proverbs 4:7). "**And examine with Understanding,**" as it is said: "On the lips of the discerning, wisdom is found." And he extends "Grace" to us like the Name.

<meta>catchword: Commentary</meta>

Both commentators emphasize that the anatomical metaphors (fingers and toes) must not be taken literally, warning against physical conceptions of God and explaining the relationship between Wisdom (Chokhmah) and Understanding (Binah).</summary>

### Ra’avad, of blessed memory — Chapter 1, Mishnah 4 — and the Rambam <note>original: הרמב; Although the header says Rambam (Maimonides), the detailed commentary below is attributed to the Ramban (Nachmanides), a common occurrence in manuscript titles where the two famous names were sometimes swapped by scribes.</note>, of blessed memory.

<margin>
Secondary version in the manuscript:
For ten Sefirot,
how many [are there]?
</margin>

**Ten and not nine; ten and not eleven.** 
This is the explanation: All the divine influences and blessings are contained within the ten Sefirot <note>The *Sefirot* are the ten attributes or "vessels" through which the Infinite God interacts with the finite world.</note>. They are ten and not nine. Do not imagine in your foolishness that Wisdom <note>original: *Hokhmah*</note> is the first attribute belonging to Him <note>The author is clarifying that above Wisdom is the "Crown" (*Keter*), the highest and most hidden Sefirah.</note>. **Ten Sefirot of Nothingness: ten and not eleven, and ten and not nine.** If you are tempted to count eleven, then **Understand with Wisdom and be wise with Understanding.** Examine them and investigate them; **set the matter on its clarity** <note>original: *borio*; meaning to understand a thing in its purest, most essential state.</note> **and restore the Creator to His established place** <note>original: *mekhono*</note>. 
Therefore it is called "His established place": He has a place of rest for Himself and before... <note>The text here enters a cryptic linguistic analysis of the words *Sela* (rock) and *Selah* (forever), arguing that any attempt to add to or subtract from the number ten results in a "deficiency" in one's understanding of the divine structure.</note>
For this reason, he says "ten and not nine" to prove the nature of the Sefirot: just as anything added is a deficiency, so too any subtraction from the Sefirot is a deficiency.

**Ten and not nine; ten and not eleven.** 
This is the explanation: All the divine influences and blessings are contained within the ten Sefirot. They are ten and not nine. Do not imagine in your foolishness that Wisdom is the first attribute belonging to Him. **Ten Sefirot of Nothingness: ten and not eleven, and ten and not nine.** If you think there are eleven, **Understand with Wisdom and be wise with Understanding.** Examine them and investigate them; **set the matter on its clarity and restore the Creator to His established place.** 
Therefore it is called "His established place": He has a place of rest for Himself and before... 
For this reason, he says "ten and not nine" to prove the nature of the Sefirot: just as anything added is a deficiency, so too any subtraction from the Sefirot is a deficiency.

### Abbreviated Commentary of the Ramban, of blessed memory

**Ten and not nine:** [Lest one think] a Sefirah is missing from the ten. They are ten, just as there are ten fingers of the hands and ten toes of the feet, and even so, the essence of the ten is neither less nor more. **Ten and not nine:** Lest you attribute power to the World <note>original: *Olam*</note>, which has length, breadth, and thickness—which comprise nine dimensions <note>Three dimensions of space across three different planes.</note>—you must also account for the "Unique Depth." Therefore they are ten. One cannot separate Wisdom <note>original: *Hokhmah*</note> from the Supreme Crown <note>original: *Keter Elyon*</note> when their essences are unified above. And when they begin to descend, one cannot separate the parts of the "Building" <note>The *Building* refers to the lower seven Sefirot that construct the manifest world.</note>. Between that which is from above to above, and that which is from above to below, there are ten. And if a person were to subtract one, the "Infinite" (*Ein Sof*) would not be included in the counting, for the "One Above" cannot be grasped... 

And here it is mentioned that one must not subtract from nor add to the ten, neither in Wisdom, nor in Understanding, nor in Knowledge. Therefore it says: **Understand with Wisdom**, for it is the foundation of Wisdom that is established above. **Be wise with Understanding**, which means to use your power of Wisdom to establish this within that. Therefore, it does not say "Understand with Understanding" and "Be wise with Wisdom," so as not to separate them. 

**Set the matter on its clarity:** Clarity <note>original: *borio*</note> refers to the pure word. Since it is the end of the thought and the brain, he gave a hint at the beginning that it is called "His clarity." All the parts of the matter are called "clarity" because they are in a straight line. **Restore the Creator to His established place:** This means to give to the "Cause of Causes" <note>A philosophical term for God as the ultimate origin.</note> the ability to return to His foundation...

It warns against confusing the Infinite Creator with His emanations and explains the cognitive process of using Wisdom and Understanding to perceive divine order.</summary>

<meta>Continuity: This page continues the commentary on Chapter 1, Mishnah 4 of Sefer Yetzirah, shifting from the general definition of the ten Sefirot to a technical and philosophical defense of why the number ten is absolute—neither nine nor eleven. It explores the correspondence between the ten Sefirot and the structures of the physical universe (the World), the calendar (the Year), and human consciousness (the Soul).</meta>

# Ra’avad, of blessed memory — Chapter 1, Mishnah 4

And they ascended in number: seven in the upper ones including the tongue, and six in the lower ones <note>original: *v-z"v b'tachtonim*; likely a scribal shorthand for the numerical arrangement of the Sefirot</note>. This means: with the "arousal" <note>The "arousal" or *m'orer* refers to the initial movement of the Divine Will.</note>. Therefore, he sat and said: "ten and not eleven," and this explains "ten and not nine." We know that the Pivot <note>original: *Goral* or *Galgal*; referring to the central axis of a system</note> does not stand alone except through the diagonals, and the pivot of all the "feet" <note>Metaphorical supports or foundations of the emanations.</note> stands only through the diagonals. 

Lest you say that the pivot is not included among the diagonals—for the number of the weeks <note>original: *shavuot*</note> is not this, and the "foot" is only nine—he comes to inform you: ten and not nine. Not nine on the right, but rather ten in the right hand and right foot. "Ten and not nine" in the left hand and left foot. "Ten and not eighteen," so that you do not think the tongue is combined with the diagonals to make seventeen, or the "arousal" is combined with the diagonals to make eighteen. Therefore it is said: "ten and not eighteen." And so too in the number ten: the diagonals are ten, and not eighteen. 

Generally, just as there is a number in the **World**, and just as there is in the **Soul**, so too in the **Year** <note>This refers to the "Olam, Shanah, Nefesh" (World, Year, Soul) triad, a fundamental concept in *Sefer Yetzirah* stating that the same divine patterns repeat in space, time, and biology.</note>. There are those who say these are the ten spheres of the Chariot <note>original: *galgaley ha-merkhavah*</note>, and some say they are the ten simple essences <note>original: *ikhuyot*</note>... diagonals. And regarding the "Year," it was not explained in all the ten because it is divided and prepared. The scholar said that the "Year" is time, and time is a concept within the movement of a specific star, its foundation, and its number. This number is measured according to the movement of the spheres. 

Each one of them has a unique movement; therefore, the Year and time are measured by every sphere and the count of its movement. For example: the Lunar Year is the time it takes for its sphere to complete a circuit, which is twenty-seven days, eight hours, and 434 parts <note>A traditional Jewish measurement of time where an hour is divided into 1080 parts (*halakim*).</note>. The Solar Year is 365 days and six hours. And so it is with every single sphere, and even with the Spheres of the Chariot. Even in this, there is "ten and not nine," so that you do not say that because the movement of the fourth sphere is not perceptible and its movement is unknown, it shouldn't be counted. Lest you say that Heaven forbid <note>original: *chas v'shalom*</note> no number applies to it at all and there are only nine Sefirot in the Year—therefore it says: "ten and not nine." And lest you say that just as the Fourth is not perceptible in number, so too the Cause of Causes <note>The ultimate source of God, the *Ein Sof*.</note> should be included in the count, making them eleven—Heaven forbid—therefore it says: "not eleven."

Regarding Wisdom <note>original: *Hokhmah*</note>, he did not say "explore all the ends of the world," because thought is forbidden from investigating there, whether in the World, the Year, or the Soul. As it is said: "Do not seek what is too wondrous for you." And our Sages, of blessed memory, said: "From one end of the heavens to the other end of the heavens you may ask," but you may not ask what is above <note>The spiritual realms</note>, what is below <note>The abyss</note>, what is before <note>Before creation</note>, or what is behind <note>The end of time</note>.

Similarly, regarding Wisdom, in the matter of the powers of the soul: we already know that the brain is divided into three parts. The front part, closest to the forehead, is the peak of spirituality, where all things are imagined... there the dreams appear. The middle part corresponds to the diagonals... within it is the power of the intellect and the understanding of sciences and all crafts. This is the "Thinking Power." And the part in the back of the head contains the "Power of Memory" and "Preservation." 

The "Soul-Spirit" <note>original: *ruach ha-nafshi*</note> resides between the membranes. There is the "Power of Imagination" and "Thought." Know that this power is the force behind all crafts. In the body, it corresponds to the "Higher Crown" <note>The Sefirah of *Keter*</note>, which contains all the powers... Wisdom, Understanding, Knowledge, Sight, Hearing, Smell, Taste, Touch, and Will. 

Lest you say that because the Will is not perceptible, there are only nine—Heaven forbid—therefore it says "ten and not nine." And lest you say the Intellect is also included in the count, it says "ten and not eleven." 

So it is with Wisdom: he did not say "investigate with the Intellect," because the Intellect is included [within the Sefirot] and no other power from the powers of the soul can be imagined within it. However, all the powers of the soul that are divided in the soul are all built and exist through the "Power of the Dwelling" <note>original: *koach ha-shekhinah*; the Divine Presence as it dwells within the soul.</note> and through Wisdom. All these nine powers are the agents of all crafts within the Power of the Shekhinah, which is the tenth among them, like a queen among them.

<sig>i ii 8 30 18</sig>
<meta>catchword: Action and night...</meta>

<summary>This page provides a philosophical and cosmological defense for why the Sefirot must number exactly ten, using the "World, Year, Soul" framework. It also outlines a medieval map of the human brain, associating different physical regions with divine attributes like Imagination, Intellect, and Memory.</summary>
<keywords>Ten Sefirot, Sefer Yetzirah, Olam Shanah Nefesh, Celestial Spheres, Human Anatomy, Shekhinah, Divine Will, Limits of Inquiry</keywords>

<meta>Continuity: This page continues the commentary on Sefer Yetzirah, Chapter 1, Mishnah 4. The previous page established the absolute nature of the number ten for the Sefirot—denying that they are nine or eleven—and began to link these emanations to the structures of the world, time, and the soul. This page moves into the specific roles of Wisdom (Chokhmah) and Understanding (Binah) in perceiving the Divine.</meta>

# Ra’avad, of blessed memory — Chapter 1, Mishnah 4 — Rambam, of blessed memory
<meta>catchword: ten</meta>

The explanation of “all that you occupy yourself with in Wisdom and Understanding” <note>original: *Chokhmah* and *Binah*; these are the second and third Sefirot, representing the spark of intuition and the analytical development of that intuition.</note> is as follows: it means you must examine, scrutinize, and stand upon the ultimate purpose and the secret of Greatness and Power <note>original: *Gedulah* and *Gevurah*; technical terms for the Sefirot of expansive Love and restrictive Strength.</note> most intensely. The end of action is found in Wisdom and Understanding, for your beloved ones and your pious ones are enslaved to investigate, observe, scrutinize, and clarify through your Wisdom. You have been refined as one refines **silver** (a metaphor) and in the testing-crucibles as one tests **gold**. 

Therefore, it said “such as they are,” meaning with the urgency found in Wisdom and Understanding. Similarly, Greatness and Power shall be enacted through Wisdom and Understanding, for they are the source from which they originate. The explanation: when you have reached the knowledge of the roots of Greatness and Power through Wisdom and Understanding, you are further obligated to investigate from Greatness and Power the kindness you have attained through Wisdom—the very edge of comprehension. This is achieved through the power of Wisdom and Understanding, according to their ability to draw out the forces from the power of Greatness and Power within them. Therefore, it said, “a source *from* them” and did not say “a source *in* them,” just as it said “such as they are.”

**Establish a matter on its clarity.** The explanation: a matter that has been clarified before Him. And for the four-letter Name <note>The Tetragrammaton, the four-letter divine name YHWH.</note>, the word “pure” etc. is used. When it is mentioned thus, the “source” is an expression of glory <note>original: *shitiya*; likely referring to the *Even Shetiya*, the Foundation Stone of the world.</note>, meaning that a matter is told to him in a vision and not as a mere command. When you reach all these levels, you must look into the pens of the Sages <note>The "pens of the Sages" refers to their written traditions and transmissions of wisdom.</note> to know its source and establish it upon its planting-place. This is why it says “foundation,” for the translation of “our planting-place” is “its clarity” <note>original: *boriyu*.</note>. **And return the Creator to His place.** The explanation: Give to Mercy <note>original: *Tiferet*; the Sefirah of Beauty/Mercy.</note>, which is called the “Creator, daughter of Understanding,” His place. These arrive at the foundation, and all these levels are within the powers of Wisdom and Understanding. And concerning the “revolving,” it says “it shall turn as a help and not as a planting.” The explanation: The Greatness is removed from the general refinement because as long as it does not stand in the Law of Kindness, it is diminished from the number **ten**. This is to warn you about the ten wisdoms, that none are missing from the number ten. In the investigation, stand and let it not be removed. This is the teaching of the Sages who clarify and ascend.

---

If you do not find as much of the world in your place, treat it as a sickness. And begin with the "Desire of Souls" and the Eternal Life <note>original: *Chayei HaNetzach*.</note>, which is proven to be refined upon the furnace. You shall establish Him and support Him upon His base. This is the matter: give your understanding to this example. For if the "Grace" <note>original: *Chen*.</note> given to the understanding were a physical body, every body would have an end, but the "Sickness" <note>In this context, likely a metaphor for the longing of the soul.</note> has no end, for its power is loftier than any accident attached to matter. And this is the meaning of "what likeness" <note>Referring to the conceptual or imaginary.</note>—"To what likeness will you compare Him?" <note>A quote from Isaiah 40:18, emphasizing God's incorporeality.</note> For He is alone in His loftiness and His remembrance. 

It is appropriate to speak of "hands" regarding something that has substance, but concerning something that has no substance, his "holiness" proves it, for the **mouth** is more holy, and the force of the **spirit** <note>original: *ruach*; can mean wind, breath, or spirit.</note> is greater. As for what is said regarding the "spirit of life within him," this refers to the formation of the body's vessels that receive the power of the spirit, which are the brain and the heart. This is especially true in the thought of the wise, the contemplative, and the one who troubles himself to think deeply. To every voice of the thinker, [He examines] every heart and all thoughts.

And all this was taught by the Great Rabbi, Rabbi Abraham, before the Academy of Babylon. And Rabbi Bahya, of blessed memory, wrote: This is the meaning of "**Prepare with Wisdom and be wise with Understanding**" in the matter of investigation. This Sefirah of **Understanding** (*Binah*) is the one that follows the Sefirah of **Wisdom** (*Chokhmah*). In the *Great Book*, it says the word "Prepare" (*hachon*) is used because preparation is more significant than existence, for preparation is the act of preparing one thing from within another. Therefore, it is associated with the Sefirah of Wisdom, to say that it was "hewn" from the Supernal Crown <note>original: *Keter Elyon*; the highest Sefirah.</note>, and from that Sefirah, the **thirty-two wondrous paths of Wisdom** branch out. All of existence is included in the great branch of the Sefirah of Wisdom, and this Sefirah is the root of everything, culminating in the thirty-two paths. And "**be wise with Understanding**" refers to the beginning of the Sefirah of Wisdom, for this Sefirah appears in action through the paths of Wisdom. If so, there is no "end of Wisdom" without Understanding; therefore he said, "Prepare with Wisdom." And there is no "end of Understanding" without Wisdom; therefore he said, "be wise with Understanding." This is the conclusion of his words.

<summary>This page provides a kabbalistic commentary on the roles of Chokhmah (Wisdom) and Binah (Understanding) as the primary tools for divine contemplation. It emphasizes God's incorporeality and explains how the ten Sefirot, specifically the 32 wondrous paths, serve as the structural blueprint for both the physical and spiritual realms.</summary>
<keywords>Wisdom (Chokhmah), Understanding (Binah), Sefirot, 32 Paths of Wisdom, Ra'avad, Rambam, Rabbi Bahya, Incorporeality, Greatness (Gedulah), Power (Gevurah)</keywords>

->To know the commentary of Nahmanides <note>original: *Ramban*; though the header identifies the commentator as Rambam (Maimonides), the text here attributes the explanation to Nahmanides. This reflects a historical debate or confusion over the authorship of this specific commentary.</note>, of blessed memory<-

This is compared to the memory that everything has an end, but these numbers have a measure and a limit, and they are ten. That measure has no end because the physical is from the sensible, the sensible from the intellectual, and the intellectual from the hidden height, and the hidden has no end. If so, according to the explanation, the physical, the intellectual, and the sensible have no end. Therefore, He wrote these qualities <note>original: *middot*</note> in order to make us understand through them that the "No End" <note>original: *Ein Sof*</note> is Depth, etc. He mentions ten depths here, as it is written: "Deep, deep, who can find it?" <note>Ecclesiastes 7:24</note>. And all the depths are in one line, even though they branch out, each one in its own way, all of them turning in their end toward the Depth of Beginning, which is Wisdom <note>original: *Chokhmah*</note>. And there is the Depth of End, which is Understanding <note>original: *Binah*</note>. And there is the Depth of Good, which is Kindness <note>original: *Chesed*</note>. And there is the Depth of Evil, which is Dread <note>original: *Pachad*, a name for the Sefirah of Gevurah or Strength</note>. Corresponding to the four fundamental elements which are included and founded within them. And afterwards, He mentions the depths of the six directions <note>original: *vav qtzavot*; the six extremities of three-dimensional space</note>, for there is nothing less than these for anything that has existence. The Depth of Height: every depth exists from the height above. And after He mentioned how much is in the hidden, he returns to mention that He has power in the revelation, for every King and Queen mentions that they have power. But He is all-high, of whom it is written: "And God alone shall be exalted" <note>Isaiah 2:11</note>. Beneath Him are the directions, and here, what is below is the Depth of Below. [The directions are] East (Beauty <note>original: *Tiferet*</note>), West (Foundation <note>original: *Yesod*</note>), North, and South. And so He built, and He is a Single Lord. After He mentioned ten boundaries in the name of the directions, He mentions six of His names against them. The name of the King who reveals Himself in the "No End" <note>original: *Ein Sof*</note> is cast within them all in His unity from His holy dwelling: Greatness, Strength, Beauty <note>original: *Gedulah, Gevurah, Tiferet*</note>, as the dwelling is the Holy One, and unto eternity of eternities from the beginning of the building until its end, which is Eternity <note>original: *Netzach*</note>, and this is over all...

<summary>This page provides a multi-layered Kabbalistic commentary on the "depths" of the Sefirot, specifically Beginning, End, Good, and Evil, emphasizing God's omnipresence and the human inability to fully grasp the divine plan. It synthesizes views from Rashi, the Zohar, and later Kabbalists like Rabbi Shabbatai Horowitz to explain how divine goodness and justice permeate all levels of existence, from the heavens to the earth.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on Chapter 1 of Sefer Yetzirah (The Book of Formation), specifically focusing on Mishnah 5, which defines the "ten depths" of the Sefirot. The previous page established the concept of the Sefirot as divine measurements; here, the text explores the infinite nature of these dimensions and their relationship to Divine Thought and the physical world.</meta>

## The Ra’avad, of blessed memory <note>Rabbi Abraham ben David of Posquières, a 12th-century legalist and mystic</note>

As part of the whole, and from it they cascade down; for the more the subject rises from level to level, the more the upper level becomes refined and purified compared to that which is below it. Thus they ascend and become more subtle until the Infinite <note>original: *Ein Sof*</note>. And because it [the Sefirah] has risen to the Cause, may He be blessed, and is not distant from any thing, it too is equal within the whole. Therefore, it taught ten [Sefirot] to indicate they have an end. **Depth of Beginning:** The explanation of "beginning" is Wisdom <note>original: *Chokmah*</note>, as it is written: "The beginning of wisdom is the fear of the Lord" (Psalm 111:10), for it is the beginning of all existing things. And it is the "end of thought" <note>A kabbalistic principle: "The end of action was first in thought"</note> like the relationship of matter to the Creator, and like the concept of Reception <note>original: *Kabbalah*</note> which is a "garland of grace" <note>Proverbs 1:9</note>, the level of the beginning of all the letters. And this is the secret of "My heart is first," and it is fitting to call it "first" because it is "something from nothing" <note>original: *yesh me-ayin*</note>. In this way, it is the first of number, for number cannot be grasped as it is understood. And because the accompaniments and the attributes fall within the definition of measurement, it was necessary to tell you: do not say He has a literal measurement; therefore, he said "Depth." **The explanation of Depth:** Every "beginning" has no measurement, and likewise, each and every one of its parts has depths, and nothing is attached to them. For all the depths are drawn from the Upper Thought; from the general depth within it are its hewings, and its quarry has no end, therefore all the depths have no end. And because it mentioned ten, it shows they have an end to say that they correspond to the Chariot <note>original: *Merkavah*, referring to the divine vision in Ezekiel</note> which is within Wisdom. He mentioned corresponding to them "depths" for every land in every sphere, and corresponding to the sphere of death <gloss>the physical world</gloss> to the "height and below" <gloss>the vertical axis</gloss> for depth. And it is known that circles have no beginning, and the measurements from the perspective of the circle are not from the perspective of its physical end. So too are the Separated Intellects <note>Angels or purely spiritual beings</note>; they are to it as matter... like the mixture of wisdom. And that which has no "how much" <note>quantity</note> cannot be said to have a beginning or an end. And this is the secret of the **Depth of Beginning**, to diminish change and a distant ascent, and [to provide] a foundation for all existing things in the way of the hidden <note>the mystical path</note>.

---

## The Ram"b, of blessed memory <note>Likely referring here to the commentary of Moses Botril, who is cited in this column</note>

To know this: Depth of Height, Depth of Below—to know to what extent His reality was, and the place of his prophecy from beneath the earth. **Depth of East and Depth of West:** He mentions East first because it is the "Face" <note>In biblical Hebrew, "East" (*Kedem*) also means "front"</note> and the West is the "Back." **Depth of North:** [He mentions it] first because it is the secret of the inhabited world, while the South is desolate. And this is what Abraham our Father said to the sages of his generation: because one cannot know His dominion, to what extent it reaches, [it is] the **Depth of Good**, the secret of the permissible, for He rules in the East, West, North, and South. But regarding the language of "good," anything outside of Him, He rules over it—meaning, "Depth of Good" teaches us that He, may He be exalted, is "before it," meaning above it, and there is nothing else before Him. And from this you may say of the good that so-and-so's voice rules over all; but of the evil, that so-and-so does not rule over it, for that is the **Depth of Evil**. And if you say this, it is as they said: all of them are ten depths corresponding to the ten Sefirot. **To a Unique Judge:** For He has no "face" <note>no human likeness or front/back distinction</note>, and this is what Solomon said: "And also both of them, do not join" <note>Ecclesiastes 7:18, interpreted here as not mixing the divine essence with these dimensions</note>. **Faithful King:** And His kingdom is for all time, and He rules in all the depths from His holy dwelling place forever and ever. "M" [for] Dwelling Place <note>original: *Ma'on*</note>—a place, as in "Lord, You have been our dwelling place" (Psalm 90:1). And likewise, "You have made the Most High your dwelling place" (Psalm 91:9). Or it means the dwelling of the "Lamp of the Eye" <note>referring to divine providence</note>, as it is explained according to the verse: "You have been a dwelling place for us," meaning the eyes of all mankind are hanging upon Him by way of the "Watcher of the Heavens," for the eye of man is toward the Lord forever. Therefore, one should not think "how much" ... Moses Botril said in *Blumah* <note>Botril's commentary on Sefer Yetzirah</note> that it is the seal of the "Place" <note>a name for God</note>, a good thing, not wisdom. And the great Rabbi, Rabbi Chasdai the Kabbalist <note>Likely Rabbi Chasdai Crescas, 1340–1410</note>, explained this: that the matter is entirely translated... a small endeavor for a great one to do... from the secrets of the one who teaches the wise man from his learning... and thus he said:

<summary>This page explores the "Ten Depths" of the Sefirot, explaining that while they define the dimensions of existence (height, depth, the four directions, good, and evil), they originate from an infinite Divine Thought that transcends measurement. The text highlights the spatial symbolism of the directions and emphasizes God's absolute unity as the "Unique Judge" who rules over these dimensions from a "holy dwelling."</summary>

<keywords>Sefer Yetzirah, Sefirot, Ra'avad, Moses Botril, Ten Depths, Chokmah, Ein Sof, Kabbalah, cosmology, Divine Thought, Merkavah</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the first chapter of Sefer Yetzirah, moving into Mishnah 8 (though the content specifically addresses the 'ten depths' described in earlier sections of the chapter). <summary>This page presents a dual commentary on Sefer Yetzirah's "ten depths" (dimensions). Ramban provides a philosophical defense of monotheism, arguing that the God of the heights is the same as the God of the depths, refuting ancient dualistic and "Chaldean" astrological theories; meanwhile, Ra'avad provides a mystical mapping of these directions to specific Sefirot and Divine Names.</summary>

<keywords>Sefirot, Ten Depths, Divine Unity, Ramban, Ra'avad, Tiferet, Yesod, Dualism, Chaldeans, Abraham the Patriarch, Divine Names, Mercy, Foundation</keywords>

<summary>This page features two parallel commentaries explaining the "ten depths" of creation. The Ramban argues for the unity of God against Chaldean dualism, while the Ra'avad explores the mystical significance of the "Depth of Evil" and the geometric nature of the celestial directions.</summary>

--- Page 100 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the ten Sefirot, shifting from their conceptual definitions to the nature of their perception and their dynamic movement. The previous page discussed the "depths" of good, evil, and direction. This page addresses Mishnah 6 of the first chapter, describing the Sefirot as a "flash of lightning" and explaining the mystical concept of oscillation known as "running and returning."</meta>

# The Commentary of the Ramban, of blessed memory, Chapter 1, Mishnah 6; The Commentary of the Ra’avad, of blessed memory

**The ten Sefirot of nothingness: their appearance is like the flash of lightning, and their end has no limit. His word is in them in running and returning <term>Ratzo v’Shuv - a concept from Ezekiel 1:14 describing the soul's oscillation between divine ecstasy and physical reality</term>, and at His command they pursue like a storm, and before His throne they prostrate themselves.**

(Right Column - The Ra'avad)
The explanation of the Great Rabbi, the Kabbalist, Rabbi Isaac ben [Abraham] <note>original: "Isaac ben Pr'"; this refers to Rabbi Isaac the Blind, a foundational figure in early Kabbalah</note>:
**The ten Sefirot of nothingness: their appearance is like the flash of lightning, and their end has no limit. His word is in them in running and returning, and at His command they pursue like a storm, and before His throne they prostrate themselves.** Look at it this way: this is an example and a secret for a person to apply his mind to them instead of his usual foundation of study, **lest he cut the shoots** <note>A Talmudic metaphor for falling into heresy while studying mystical secrets</note> in the vacuum and fall defeated. Therefore, he chose to speak of the ten Sefirot and the engagement with His word as something that **runs and returns**. This power and spirit from the attribute of the *Chayot* <note>The "Living Creatures" or angels from Ezekiel's vision of the Divine Chariot</note> do what they do through "running and returning," for they are like souls in their stature. So too shall the person who investigates this wonderful secret do: he should seek to investigate this with clear wine <note>A metaphor for pure, refined intellect</note>, for the King cannot be contained. For their end has no limit, but is broad like the nature of the sea. They do the will of their Creator and pursue after Him like a storm. Thus—

<column-break/>

(Left Column - The Ram"b <note>Likely referring here to the "Rabbi of the Binah," a title sometimes associated with the school of Ramban or his disciples</note>)
And from their wings "like the flash of lightning." This refers to the bodies of the perceptions of the Sefirot, bodies that are woven into one another. And the "appearance" there is like the flash of lightning, like the light that emerges from between shards <note>Original: "cherasim." The author compares divine light to light glimpsed through the cracks of broken pottery</note>. And the explanation of this: if you take a sieve that has very fine holes across its entire surface, and you place a shard over it and blow on it slightly until it ignites, and the light passes through the paths of the holes—in every single hole, flames appear as if they are gazing out, in all the colors of the world, descending through the paths of the holes. So too are the wings of the *Chayot* in the paths of those *Chayot* which are above them. For there is a Throne for the Throne and Glory for the Glory. They are in their wings, and in the language of the intermediate [world] it is described as various kinds of properties. Out of the fear of their reach, they are perceived immediately as "running and returning." They are perceived like a flash of lightning, which appears and disappears in the blink of an eye, or like threads that rise and fall in the blink of an eye. From here, the masters of the Secret Name <note>original: "Ba'alei Hashem" - mystics and wonder-workers</note> said: Do not look at anything except that which looks at you. And immediately look back to the ground... and covered thus, and covered—

---

->The Commentary of the Ramban <note>Rabbi Moses ben Nahman, 13th-century scholar</note>, of blessed memory<-

**Their appearance:** The perception itself is its "body" within them. **Like the flash of lightning:** It does not say "it is the appearance" but "like the appearance." Likewise, "and a portion in the eye" <note>likely a reference to visual perception</note> is only for contemplating and looking at a thing to satisfy the researcher's knowledge. **The lightning:** This is like that which is found in the [heavenly] furnace. **And their end:** That which is included in the flash, for it is without end. For their end has no limit; any defined thing that has a measure is said to have an "end," but anything that has no measurable scale is considered "infinite." **His word is in them in running and returning:** The explanation is that the one who delights in them raises his perception to look at their light and their whiteness with longing; but the one who looks at the Exalted and at each one individually and does not stand still in his perception is called "His word" which is above them. He rises to receive the command of the Will in extra-abundant sources, in "running and returning." **And at His command:** For those who receive the teaching of longing also **pursue like a storm.** **And before His throne they prostrate themselves:** This is the turning of the Will toward the Face <note>The inner aspect of the Divine</note>. All their "going out" is the going out of the power sent from above to below. My language here refers to the receiving of the "face" [presence], for their bodies were in it in order to receive His word. And its "life" <note>or vitality</note> is the power sent from the Word to the messenger. It is like the metaphor of a scribe: the words draw the power to the author through the speed of the creation. The Word and the messenger **prostrate before His throne**—which means to behold the Glory—they bow down.

<summary>This page explores the paradoxical nature of perceiving the Sefirot, using the metaphor of a "flash of lightning" to describe how divine light is both revealed and concealed. It warns of the dangers of mystical study while explaining the "running and returning" (oscillation) of the soul and the angels as they pursue the infinite Divine Will.</summary>
<keywords>Sefirot, lightning, running and returning, Isaac the Blind, Ramban, Chayot, divine throne, mystical perception, infinity</keywords>
